- 2026-10-23 · 1.4a Spike: rendering and posting · 751442b · in the 17.7.1 sandbox with Umbraco's website output cache on: a view component, `<jfb-render>` and `<jfb-assets>` render two forms per page with ids jfb-1 and jfb-2; the no-JS post goes through surface routing (one form opened with `BeginUmbracoForm(…, antiforgery: false)`, one with our own form element and `SurfaceControllerHiddenInput`), re-renders errors through `CurrentUmbracoPage()` and redirects to `?jfb-sent=<instance>#<instance>`; the JS post goes to `/jfb/api/v1` with the challenge fetched on first interaction, a 422 with a lazily imported error chunk, an idempotent replay and a 409 `form_changed` answered by swapping in the published form from `render` with the answers kept. Playwright in Edge, 70 checks each against the `dotnet build` site (Development) and the `dotnet publish` site (Production, assets from the published wwwroot): JavaScript disabled and enabled, errors and success on both, two forms keeping their own errors and values, the cached page byte-identical (4,435 bytes) for three visitors with different cookies and holding no token, nonce or challenge, no CSP violation with `script-src 'self'`, a nonce, or a nonce with `'strict-dynamic'`, and the expected block of a nonce on a cached page. Found: the cache ignores the query string (without the spike's vary rule the outcome came back as the cached form: 64 of 70), its policy checks neither method nor status (posts were still never stored), `CurrentPage` threw `500` on the auto-route (fixed to `400`). A 50-field form renders in a median of 0.12 to 0.15 ms. ADR-0004 and docs/research/spike-rendering.md. - 2026-10-23 · 1.5 Spike: one logic, two runtimes · 106a893 · a minimal evaluator for conditions, rules, stored numbers and calculations in C# (.NET 10.0.12) and TypeScript (Node 24.21.0 with ICU 78.3), run by `dotnet test` (NUnit) and `npx vitest run` against one file of 163 vectors (`jfb-vectors/1`, written as ASCII from code points): Vitest 164 of 164, NUnit 171 of 171, both reading `.local/sandbox/vectors/spike.json`. Comparing every code point: case mappings differed on 55 letters added in Unicode 16 and 17 (.NET on Windows cases through the host's ICU), so folding is limited to the Latin, Greek, Cyrillic and Armenian blocks and the full-width Latin letters; .NET's `Normalize` threw for U+FFFE and its JSON reader for lone surrogates, so clean replaces them and every noncharacter; 20 Unicode 16 compositions are listed exactly as known gaps. Native `RegExp` took 98 ms for `(a+)+` on 24 characters, doubling with each one, so patterns run on an own Thompson-automaton matcher in both runtimes (100,000 characters in 2 to 35 ms). Exact decimals replace `decimal` and doubles (division to 20 places, `round` half away from zero). The broken operator (`greaterThan` made inclusive) failed `number-greater-than-equal-is-false` and was reverted; the first 115 vectors had all passed it, so mutation sweeps followed (37 of 61 killed at first) and 48 vectors were added: now 73 of 74 TypeScript mutants (the survivor is equivalent in JavaScript) and 14 of 14 C# mutants are killed. With invariant globalization 13 C# tests fail, so start-up will refuse it (4.4). ADR-0005 and docs/research/spike-logic.md. - 2026-10-23 · 1.6 Spike: background work and email · 86d24fb · in the 17.7.1 sandbox (SQLite, WAL): action runs and mail shaped like §4.5, each claimed by one conditional update with a token of its own; one worker per server woken after the commit through a scoped notification (delivered after the outermost commit, never after a rollback) and a bounded channel of one; a 1-minute distributed job that only wakes the worker; mail through `IEmailSender` to the pickup folder, gated by `CanSendRequiredEmail()`. From the commit to the mail: median 7.8 ms, p99 12.1 ms over 200 submissions, 8.3 ms 20 s after start before Umbraco's runner started, bursts of 20 and 50 all sent within 141 and 348 ms; with the job alone 1.7 to 60.7 s. Umbraco's runner waits for each job (a job that slept 70 s held every other distributed job), and a 5-second job ran about every 20 s. Five simulations of 1,000 runs on two processors (the second with its own SQLite connections) with refusals, cut-offs, slow attempts and crashes: 0 runs and 0 of 1,788 mails with two effects, 0 violations; mutants caught (claim without its state check 25 doubles, look-then-claim 8, expired leases back to pending 12), and read-then-write in one transaction hung the database. Pause: nothing sent while paused, all 10 within 74 ms of resuming. Mail has one body (HTML or text; no multipart/alternative), so ADR-0007 sends HTML by default and plain text when the action chooses it. Research in `docs/research/spike-background.md`.

- 2026-10-23 · roadmap change · from the Phase 1 review (1.8). **Facts confirmed:**
  - §3.5's Marketplace categories, from the schema read on 2026-10-23: eleven categories, none for forms, so "Editor Tools" plus "Campaign & Marketing" stands. The field list gains `VersionDependencyMode`, the `PackageType` values and the two deprecated fields.
  - 18.1's appsettings schema is merged by Umbraco.Cms.Targets' `UmbracoJsonSchemaFiles` (17.6.2, 17.7.1 and 18.2.1), so 18.3's smoke test checks the site's reference.
  - §3.4 names the step that confirmed each fact.

  **Conflicts fixed:** two subagent reviews of the ADRs against §1, §2 and §4 found 37 conflicts (four found by both), a check of those fixes found 18 more, a check of that round found 13 more, two further checks found 5 each, and a last one found 2. Each was verified, then fixed where it lives:
  - D12 drops Umbraco's sorter (ADR-0016).
  - D14: rate limits refuse with `429` and `Retry-After` rather than score (ADR-0012 refines it).
  - D21's full-major deprecation now binds ADR-0010 and §4.14: removal at the major after the next.
  - D13's "no cookies" holds. Members-only forms bind posts to the member through their challenge instead of an antiforgery cookie, with same-origin credentials, framing refused, and defined no-JS posts (ADR-0004, ADR-0012).
  - Double opt-in is a form setting, not an action. An unverified entry runs no action.
  - §1.1 describes file storage as ADR-0006 built it, and D8 names the draft-version cleanup.
  - Consent:
    - consent texts recall no answers;
    - the form posts the hash of the wording it showed;
    - a consent whose wording changed is never re-ticked.
  - Sensitive fields and `includeInEmails` get one rule per output.
  - The privacy section is global. Logs hold no IP addresses either. The server key's name is a reserved context value.
  - The honeypot is hidden by base rules that every theme and the headless element ship, and its name cannot be an alias.
  - The permission matrix:
    - `403` for a missing permission;
    - `404` where the user holds nothing on the resource;
    - the section requirement tested with a user who lacks the section;
    - the form picker as its one exception.
  - Public API:
    - `no-store` except the definition;
    - `invalid_request` for `400`;
    - `422 validation_failed` everywhere;
    - full `validate` and `drafts` bodies, and drafts keep no files;
    - a rate-limited no-JS post keeps its answers.
  - Mail and settings:
    - the final `Limited` mail state, with its purge;
    - the `+tag` rule for recipient keys;
    - grants below a restricted folder use ADR-0013's wording;
    - no host defaults for backoffice settings (`AllowImplicitCaptchaConsent` replaces the consent-mode key, and `DefaultTheme` goes).
  - Schema and themes:
    - themes are linked by alias, with one home each for the default theme and the default email layout;
    - `TextKey` is folded as rules compare;
    - draft-only catalogue rows have no `FirstVersionNumber`.
  - Logic:
    - unsafe patterns are refused at publish;
    - all three `form_changed` conditions are named;
    - the rounded calculation result is the value everywhere;
    - the error kinds of ADR-0002 are listed;
    - consent is required by default, and cards show option images;
    - a run waits while an earlier one is running.
  - The submission pipeline (§4.4, now nine steps):
    - the gate (on a members-only form the origin first, then rate limits, the challenge and its member) comes first, then idempotency, before the revision, availability and the challenge's single use, so a retry after a lost answer gets its receipt;
    - a known `submissionId` is answered from its entry only for that entry's challenge or server key; a retry whose challenge has expired asks the receipt route, which accepts the challenge for 24 hours past its expiry, and past that grace the fresh post is shown as already sent;
    - the members-only surface action checks the origin before any re-render;
    - values kept "until the actions have run" survive while a trigger can still fire (`Unverified`, `Spam`, waiting for approval), and every deletion rule says so;
    - "not spam" sends an unverified entry of a verification form back to `Unverified`;
    - at confirmation, the runs keep the order shown.
  - One consent hash (`jfbConsentText`) in the markup, the projection and every submission; drafts keep no consent answers.
  - "Create a form block type" needs `manageSettings` and Umbraco's Settings section, and an outsider persona tests the section requirement.
  - `ufprt` is a reserved alias, and the markup contract lists the members-only challenge input.

  **Checks added:** 5.3, 6.1, 6.2, 6.3, 6.4, 6.6, 6.7, 7.1, 8.2, 8.5, 8.6, 9.5b, 14.3 and 18.3. None was weakened. Two cases moved, unchanged, to the step that builds what they test: 5.3's storage case to 6.1, and 7.1's "not spam" to `Unverified` to 8.5. Step 8.5 is renamed "Content and state actions, email verification and approval", and the ADRs cite the new pipeline step numbers.

### Handover after Phase 1 (2026-10-23)
- **State:** Phase 1 is complete, and nothing is half-done.
  - **Research:** the reference inventories and the feature catalogue (1.1).
  - **The six spikes** (1.2a to 1.6), in the git-ignored `.local/sandbox/`. Their code is throwaway; what they proved is in `docs/research/`.
  - **ADRs** 0001–0017, 0019 and 0020. ADR-0018 (the AI add-on) belongs to 15.x.
  - **`docs/architecture.md`.**
  - **§3 and §4** hold no *(verify)*. 4.1's offline SQL Server check is that step's own feasibility question.
  - **The phase review** changed several ADRs (each status line says what), and §1 and §2 only where an ADR now says so.
- **Wiring sweep:** Phase 1 added no product code, so there is no setting, endpoint, notification, extension point or menu item to wire yet. Its one script, `scripts/sandbox.mjs` (18 tests), served every spike, and 3.3's demo script takes over its pattern. What the spikes proved, and the step that builds it:
  - the backoffice shapes: 9.x (`docs/research/umbraco-backoffice.md`);
  - migrations and the entry query shapes: 4.1, 4.3;
  - rendering, the no-JS post and the output cache: 6.1, 6.2, 6.4;
  - uploads and the file store: 4.3, 6.1, 7.3, 15.2;
  - the vectors and the pattern matcher: 3.4, 5.2, 5.3;
  - the outbox and the kick after commit: 8.1, 8.2.
- **Next:** 2.1, information architecture and words. No product code in Phase 2. What it needs:
  - the role matrix follows ADR-0013's eleven permissions and its personas (designer, entry handler, viewer, administrator, privacy officer, no access, and the outsider without the section);
  - the section's menus follow 9.1 and §4.13 (D18: one primary action per view, say it once);
  - the vocabulary of CLAUDE.md;
  - the last section of `docs/research/umbraco-backoffice.md`;
  - a subagent that plays a first-time editor checks the click paths;
  - the three design skills apply from 2.2 on (ADR-0019, ADR-0020).
- **Gotchas:**
  - `cd` into a reference folder moves this session's working directory; it happened twice. Use `git -C` and absolute paths.
  - Bash once failed to parse a long heredoc full of quotes. Write longer edit scripts with the file tool (no `\u` escapes there) and apply them through a helper that requires each replacement to match exactly once.
  - Most review findings were drift. An ADR refined a decision, but other passages kept the old wording. After any decision change, grep every doc for the old wording.
  - GitHub tables split on a pipe even inside a code span, so escape it (`\|`).
  - The reviews found more each round, so a check of the fixes themselves was worth it: six rounds, from 37 findings down to 2. Fixes drift too (one round dropped "the same body" from a check), so give every round of fixes its own narrow check.
  - The PC crashed once during the review. `git fsck` was clean and all commits were there. A background subagent cut off by a crash must be started again.
- **Open owner items added:** none.

- **Gotchas:**
  - The PC crashed during 2.4. Commits were safe, uncommitted work stayed on disk, and a background subagent cut off by the crash resumes from its transcript with SendMessage (tell it to re-read its files first). A background shell job is simply lost: run it again.
  - `node scripts/wireframes.mjs` and `node scripts/website-mocks.mjs` without a filter delete every PNG and render them all again; pass a page name while working.
  - Heredocs in Bash drop backslashes (a regex in a helper script lost them), and the file tools decode `\uXXXX`. Write scripts with the Write tool and keep backslashes out of them where a character class will do.
  - Headless Edge has no German hyphenation dictionary, so `hyphens: auto` does nothing there; long German words need room or folding.
  - The impeccable detector compares the backoffice with `DESIGN.md`, which describes the website form, so its colour advisories on the wireframes are expected; its `cramped-padding` hits are wrappers with fixed heights. The checkers' own measurements are the gate.
  - Parallel subagents never share a file: one owned kit.css (its canvas rules), the others used page-local styles, and the main session made the other kit changes. That kept four agents from overwriting each other.
- **The gate:** `secret-scan --tracked` and `check-text --tracked` clean; `term-guard --tracked` clean (0 patterns: no term list here), plus a scan of tracked and new files and every commit message for the 20 product names in the private notes: 0 hits (the same patterns find 40 lines in the notes); no `[~]` step to retry; `.private/` holds only `research/feature-study.md` and no mapping request; `ci-local.ps1`, the Playwright suite and coverage start in 3.5, 3.4 and Phase 4.

- 2026-10-23 · 3.4b Vector runners and the tables check · 49da93b · **The logic** (ADR-0030): the spike's modules of 1.5 ported to Core's `Logic/` (internal, visible to Web and the unit tests: `TextSemantics`, `ExactDecimal`, `CalendarValues`, `SafePattern`, `Rules`, `Calculations`, `LogicErrors`) and to `packages/logic/src` (`errors`, `text`, `decimal`, `dates`, `pattern`, `rules`, `calc`, exported from `index.ts`; `.ts` imports with `allowImportingTsExtensions` in the base configuration and erasable syntax only, so Node runs the tables tool on the sources). Reading each line against its twin found seven problems, now fixed in both runtimes: no bound on the pattern automaton (`((^){1000}){1000}` was a million instructions, and the C# weight wrapped to -727,379,968 for four nested `{1000}`), so a second limit of 10,000 instructions with both counts capped; TypeScript lookups answered from `Object.prototype` ("constructor", "toString", "valueOf", "hasOwnProperty"), now `Map`s and `own()`; null operands crashed the TypeScript check; `DateTimeOffset.TryParse` and `new Date` read the instant in different forms, a zone-less one as local time, so one strict form from 1970 to 9998; Windows time zone names (.NET) and offsets (browsers) are now `unknown_time_zone`; trailing fraction zeros cost one division each; and `toWellFormed` is ES2024. **The vectors:** `tests/vectors/build.mjs` writes each module of `tests/vectors/suites/` as ASCII `jfb-vectors/1` (`--check`; 8 script tests): `spike.json` is the 1.5 file byte for byte (163 cases, `cmp` identical), `port.json` holds 48 cases from the review, of which the spike's TypeScript fails 24 and its C# 12 (25 together; the nested-count case was not run on the spike's C#, which would allocate without bound). **The runners:** NUnit `VectorTests` (`Vector(<suite>/<id>)`, every `tests/vectors/*.json` read in place through `RepositoryRoot`) and Vitest `packages/logic/test/vectors.test.ts`. **The tables check:** `npm run tables` writes `tests/vectors/tables/typescript.json` (Node 24.21.0, ICU 78.3, Unicode 17.0: 728 folds, 3,258 cleaned, 13,253 recompositions, identical to the spike's), a Vitest test keeps it current, and `UnicodeTablesTests` finds 0 differences for fold and clean and none in recomposition beyond the 20 known Unicode 16 gaps (.NET 10.0.12). Done when: NUnit runs both files with every case passing (211 vectors) and the tables check passes; `dotnet test Jiron.Umbraco.FormBuilder.slnx --blame-hang-timeout 10m` passes 313 unit and 17 integration tests; the client's `npm test` passes 244 (218 in `@jfb/logic`), `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck` and `npm run build` pass; 174 root script tests pass; `dotnet build -c Release --no-incremental` 0 warnings; `dotnet format --verify-no-changes`, check-text, the tracked secret scan and the name scan are clean. Also: ADR-0005 and §4.3.3 amended, §3.4 gained the platform facts, 5.2 and 5.3 now limit how deeply groups and calculations nest (both evaluators recurse); the 3.4a line's wording about another address corrected. Commit 91e5e6e names `tsconfig.tools.json`, which 737b6db adds, so `npm run typecheck` needs both. A one-off `dotnet run` of a file-based program for the time zone facts left the CLI's own build cache under `%TEMP%\dotnet\runfile`; such checks use a project under `%TEMP%\jfb-*` from now on.

- 2026-10-23 · 3.5 CI, the local CI mirror and phase review · 14d3f5a · **CI** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, ADR-0032): four jobs on ubuntu-latest, with the actions' current majors read from their release pages (checkout v7, setup-dotnet v6, setup-node v7, cache v6, upload-artifact v7). `build` runs the repository checks, the script tests on Playwright's Chromium, the client's lint, tests, build and size budgets, restore, a Release build, `dotnet format`, the NuGet licences and the notices, the packs, and the SQLite tests with trx results, which it uploads with the packages. `sqlserver` runs the integration tests against a `mssql/server:2022-latest` service whose password is made from the run id. `smoke` runs the smoke project in Chromium and uploads the report, the traces and the host's log. `loadbalancing` starts SQL Server and creates the shared database `JfbLoadBalancing`; it runs the `lb` project once `tests/e2e/specs/lb/` exists, and until then ends with a notice that names 8.1. Pushes to `main` and `v18/main` run nothing until `vars.JFB_CI == 'true'`; runs by hand and `v*` tags always run; `NUGET_PACKAGES` points restore and the notices at one folder; a newer push cancels the older run. `.github/dependabot.yml`: nuget, the three npm projects and github-actions, weekly, limit 0. **The checks:** `check-workflows.mjs` (GitHub's MIT parser plus the gate rules, both release lines in a push trigger on branches, Dependabot over every npm lock file); `check-npm-licences.mjs` (rule 4's shipped list unless the lock marks an entry `dev`, then also the development list and ADR-0027; tested with MIT-0, Unlicense, CC0-1.0 and "BSD 3-Clause" accepted in a development package, a dedication refused in one that could ship, and `UNLICENSED`, an empty licence and the forbidden families refused everywhere); `check-audit-suppressions.mjs` (a direct reference to a suppressed package, any direct ImageSharp reference, an ImageSharp entry outside the host-only group and a suppression outside an ItemGroup fail, each tested); `check-nuget-licences.mjs` (new: shipped, dev and platform tiers from every restore graph, 342 packages of which 0 ship, 16 are dev and 326 are Umbraco's platform; NUnit's MIT licence file is the one reviewed exception, pinned by its SHA-256); `third-party-notices.mjs` (writes the shipped table and the list of the four ADR-0027 packages, and checks the development table against the restored versions and licences, which added `@actions/workflow-parser`, `yaml` and `tests/e2e/`; it reads `NUGET_PACKAGES` or the default cache, with a test for each). Shared helpers: `scripts/lib/licences.mjs`, an SPDX reader (Tandem's regular expression would have passed `(MIT OR GPL-3.0-only) AND (SSPL-1.0 OR AGPL-3.0-only)`), `scripts/lib/nuget.mjs` and `scripts/lib/browser.mjs` (Edge locally, Chromium in CI, for the five places that opened Edge). **The gate:** `scripts/ci-local.ps1` runs the repository checks, the client, .NET, the packages, the tests and the browser projects, judged by exit code only, and prints the time per stage. Its browser stage runs `scripts/e2e-gate.mjs`, which reruns the projects once only when a host left the hang marker in that run (7 tests; an unknown project failed at once with exit 1 and no rerun). `pack.mjs --no-build` now packs the version the build stamped. **The phase review** is in the handover note below. Done when: `scripts/ci-local.ps1` passed all six stages in 185 s (repository checks 10.1 s with 250 script tests, client 40.9 s with 244 tests, .NET 18.2 s with 0 warnings and 0 errors, packages 2.0 s, tests 12.4 s with 321 unit and 20 integration tests, browser 101.9 s with 21 of 21 smoke tests after a 62 s start-up wait); `node scripts/check-workflows.mjs` printed "ci.yml: ok (4 jobs)" and "dependabot.yml: ok"; OWNER-TODO's CI item now reads "set `JFB_CI`, raise the Dependabot limit, and check the first CI run (including the SQL Server and load-balancing jobs)", merging the two earlier items; pushed, with the phase-review gate below. An earlier gate run, before `e2e-gate.mjs` took over the rerun from PowerShell, also passed (201 s). No process of these runs is left.

### Handover after Phase 3 (2026-10-23)
- **State:** Phase 3 is complete, and nothing is half-done.
  - **The solution (3.1):** `Jiron.Umbraco.FormBuilder.slnx` with Core, Web and the meta package, empty composers, central versions with Umbraco `[17.5.0, 18.0.0)`, and `NuGetAudit.props` (ADR-0024, ADR-0025).
  - **The client (3.2):** the npm workspace with logic, runtime, element and backoffice, the licence banner, JFB001 and the size check (ADR-0026, ADR-0027).
  - **The demo (3.3):** the Lindenhall Library on 5500, `scripts/demo.ps1` and `scripts/demo-check.mjs` (ADR-0028).
  - **The tests (3.4a to 3.4c):** Tests.Unit, Tests.Integration and the Testing project (ADR-0029), the vectors and the tables check (ADR-0030), and the browser harness on 5520 (ADR-0031).
  - **CI and the gate (3.5):** `ci.yml`, `dependabot.yml`, the five checks and `ci-local.ps1` (ADR-0032).
- **The review:**
  - **Build files:** one duplicate, removed: Core granted its internals to Tests.Unit a second time, which `src/Directory.Build.props` already does. `Directory.Packages.props` no longer restates licences, which the notices and the checks now own. Everything else has one home: shared settings in `Directory.Build.props`, package metadata in `src/Directory.Build.props`, exact sibling versions in `src/Directory.Build.targets`, the test SDK in `tests/Directory.Build.props`, versions in `Directory.Packages.props`, and `JfbUmbracoHost` only in the two site hosts.
  - **Pack contents** (`.local/ci-packages`, 17.0.0-alpha.0, listed from the zips): the meta package holds only `lib/net10.0/_._`, `LICENSE.md` and its nuspec, and depends on Core and Web at `[17.0.0-alpha.0]` and on `Umbraco.Cms.Web.Website [17.5.0, 18.0.0)`. Core holds its DLL and XML docs. Web holds its DLL and XML docs, the static web asset props and `App_Plugins/JironFormBuilder/` (`jfb-backoffice.js`, `umbraco-package.json`); its DLL embeds the six website files (`jfb/assets/runtime/jfb-runtime.js` and `jfb/assets/element/jfb-element.js`, each with `.br` and `.gz`). Every packed `LICENSE.md` matches the root one (SHA-256 1fab54a9...), licence acceptance is required, no source map, test assembly or client source is packed, and Core and Web come with `.snupkg` symbols. The "missing a readme" message stays until 17.x and 18.1.
  - **Audit suppressions** (ADR-0024): `dotnet list package --vulnerable --include-transitive` still shows all 17 advisories at the 17.5.0 floor or at 17.7.1, so every entry stays, and the build shows no new one.
- **Wiring sweep:** Phase 3 added no product setting, endpoint, notification, extension point or menu item: Core's and Web's composers stay empty until Phase 4. What it added, and the test that shows each one's effect:
  - the test-only settings of `JironFormBuilderTesting`: `Endpoints` (`TestEndpointsHostTests`, and `/jfb-testing/status` in the smoke project), `Clock` (`FormBuilderBootTests` gets the test clock; the smoke status test), `StubPort` (`StubHostedServiceTests`; the smoke webhook test), `StartupMarker` (`StartupMarkerTests`, `Boot_WithoutTheStartupMarker_RegistersNoMarkerPopulator`) and the people (`TestPeopleTests`, every persona's sign-in in the smoke project);
  - the test-only endpoints `/jfb-testing/status` and `/jfb-testing/clock`, and the stub's `/webhooks/*`, captcha `siteverify` paths and `/_stub/` (the unit tests of the gate and the stub; `TestEndpoint_OnAHostOutsideDevelopment_IsNotThere`);
  - the notification handlers on `UmbracoApplicationStartedNotification`: `TestPeopleSeeder` and the demo's seeder (the integration and smoke tests; `demo-check.mjs`);
  - the demo's settings: the imaging key it refuses to start without, `Demo:Members` and `Umbraco:CMS:DeliveryApi:Enabled` (`demo-check.mjs`; the smoke test of the Delivery API, run with the switch off and on);
  - the client's `window.JironFormBuilder` (`jfb-runtime.test.ts`), the `<jfb-form>` element (`jfb-element.test.ts`) and the backoffice manifest with telemetry off (`bundle.manifests.test.ts`);
  - the build: JFB001 (the stamp tests and the pack check), the embedded website assets and the exact sibling versions (both in the pack listing above);
  - the switches: `JFB_E2E_DELIVERY_API` (both smoke runs of 3.4c), `JFB_E2E_SKIP_BUILD` and `JFB_E2E_CONFIGURATION` (the gate's browser stage logs the first, and `host.test.ts` checks the Release path), `NUGET_PACKAGES` (the notices tests), `JFB_CI` (the workflow check) and `CI` (`browser.test.mjs` and the browser tests' config).

Then the entry takes its files (uploads of its form that no entry took, `MissingFile`), checks that every value names a field of the form's catalogue (`MissingField`), and inserts its values 100 rows to a statement; a refusal anywhere rolls the unit back, the number included. Values fill at most one typed column; `textKey` (`EntryTextKey`) is `TextSemantics.Fold(TextSemantics.Clean(text))` for text-only values, cut to 256 code units never inside a surrogate pair. Values of many entries load at most 1,000 ids to a statement. A state changes only from the state the user saw, and an update that changes nothing is always a conflict, also when a later read finds that state again; a file's scan state works the same way. Files: recorded once per key for a form that exists, orphans oldest first, deleted only from where they were seen (`FormBuilderConcurrencyException` when an entry took the orphan meanwhile) and while no value names them (`InUse`). Consent wordings: `ConsentTextHash` is the SHA-256 of the culture's UTF-8 bytes, a zero byte and the text's, stored once; a unique refusal from a writer at the same moment is the answer. `IEntryEventRepository` appends events (timeline by `occurredUtc`, then id) and `IEntryNoteRepository` adds, reads, changes and deletes notes; both hold the entry's row first, insert, and read the new row back by its id. The page is a site path (one slash, not two or a slash and a backslash, no query or fragment). **Guards are locking reads** (`SqlNames.LockingRead`, the same `UPDLOCK, HOLDLOCK` as the insert-once checks): in an entry's unit, and in every repository of 4.3a too (a form's delete, a draft version's prune, which now reads its own form by id, a catalogue field's delete and add, a form's folder, a folder's delete and restore), so on SQL Server under read-committed snapshot a writer at the same moment meets the repository's refusal, not a foreign key's error; only the theme in use stays a plain read (ADR-0037). `Architecture/SqlGuardTests` reads the repositories' source and fails on an `EXISTS` check or a count of named ids without the hint, because the hints are empty on SQLite. Done when: CRUD for each repository (`EntryRepositoryTests` 32, `EntryEventRepositoryTests` 5, `EntryNoteRepositoryTests` 4) and `FiftyParallelInserts_GetTheNumbers1To50_EachOnce`; `EntryTextKeyTests` folds as the rule vectors of 1.5 compare, with the Turkish i's, final sigma and no folding outside the folded blocks. **Review:** a subagent read the code against ADR-0003, ADR-0036 and ADR-0038 and found nine points, all fixed before the first commit: the 50 parallel inserts are serialised by the SQLite gate, so a test now shows the counter is the unit's first statement and the insert takes its number; a state change could succeed silently after the state had cycled; SQL Server's case-insensitive collation can equate text keys that rules keep apart (4.3d now compares them again in a binary collation, with a Done-when check for `straße` and `strasse` on both databases); the orphan cleanup could delete a file an entry had just taken; raw foreign-key errors under read-committed snapshot (the locking reads); one combined `NOT EXISTS` would scan instead of seeking each index; the page could name another host; the ADR did not say the counter leaves the form's concurrency version alone; missing tests (a replace with the entry's own file, the backstops, counter checks on every refusal, timelines by time). **Mutation check:** 82 behaviours put back wrong (the counter and its first place, each condition of the insert and its classification, the backstop's inner error, files taken, values, chunks and order, text keys, states and scans with the cycle, deletes, orphans, the file owner, consent hashing and its backstop, the page rules, events, notes, and each kind of locking read with the source scan); three first did not build (deleting the only call of a private method, an expression the analyser wants static), were rewritten to keep the code building, and all 82 were caught. Also: `scripts/ci-local.ps1` passed all six stages (298 s: 1,057 unit and 204 integration tests, 244 client tests, the browser projects 21 of 21); the naming check (`dotnet format style --diagnostics IDE1006`) clean; secret scan and check-text clean on every commit (a test URL with a user and password and a `…` in a code comment were caught by the gate's first run and fixed); name scan 0 hits in added lines. **Roadmap:** 4.3d gains the binary-collation bullet and its Done-when check, and 4.5 a pointer to the guard rule, both from this review. **Gotchas:** an NPoco `IExecutingInterceptor` that rewrites a statement's text (`CommandRewriter`) is the way to show a unique index's backstop and a zero-row update deterministically on SQLite; Umbraco's SQL Server test databases are made with a plain `CREATE DATABASE`, without read-committed snapshot, so the CI job cannot show a missing locking read either, hence the source scan.
- 2026-10-24 · 4.3d The entries query builder · 81ce702 · **The query builder** (ADR-0039, internal `IEntryQueryRepository` over `EntryQuerySql`): typed filters per field (a text one of or none of up to 50 values, a prefix shorter than the key, a number or date range with open or closed bounds, checked or not, answered or not; up to 10 filters, each true for any answer of the field) become the shapes of ADR-0003 only: `IN` and `NOT IN` subqueries over the value indexes, keyset pages by submission (`(submittedUtc, id)` along the form's indexes) or by a value (the walk `FROM jfbEntryValue v CROSS JOIN jfbEntry e WHERE e.id = +v.entryId`, each entry at its first answer by row and then item, then the tail of the entries without one, newest first), and a search over the entry's search text that counts up to 1,000 and reads the first page by newest first from the same 1,001 rows. A text compares by its key with the column's collation for the seek and then a binary `LIKE` without wildcards, which keeps apart what SQL Server's case-insensitive collation equates (a sharp s and `ss`) and pads nothing; a key that may be cut reads the field's distinct full texts with that key, folds them in C# and filters on those that match; a prefix is one `LIKE` on SQLite, which ignores collations, folds ASCII letters only and seeks the `NOCASE` index, and the binary `LIKE` alone on SQL Server, whose collations may read letters as one. A number bound is the double its digits parse to, as stored numbers are. A cursor's row is read back once (`IFNULL`, `ISNULL`) and only as a row of the form or a value of the sort's field, so a deleted row's position still holds and a cursor never places a page by another form's data. Refused before the database: more than 10 filters or 50 values, a prefix the key may cut, a cursor of another sort, more than 2,000 parameters. **The budget** (`FormBuilderDatabase.QueryAsync`, `FormBuilderQueryBudget`): every list is one read under 10 s; on SQLite the progress handler (SQLitePCLRaw's, found by name on the provider connection inside Umbraco's wrappers, as Core references no client) checks the deadline every 10,000 steps and is cleared before the connection goes back, on SQL Server a linked token cancels the command; past the deadline the read ends with `FormBuilderQueryTimeoutException` (never rerun, not transient), a caller's cancellation stays a cancellation, a refusal stays a refusal, and a budgeted read inside another keeps the outer budget. Unit tests (EntryQuerySqlTests and the budget's paths in FormBuilderDatabaseTests) pin the SQL per database, the placeholder order, every refusal, the timeout, cancellation and refusal paths and the nested budget; `Architecture/QueryBuilderReadsTests` fails on any database call of the query builder but its one budgeted read. **Review:** a subagent read the code against ADR-0003, ADR-0038 and ADR-0039 and found four points and gaps, all fixed before the first commit: a cursor could read another form's row or another field's value; on SQL Server a short key could equal a longer, cut key that ends in spaces (`=` and `IN` pad, `LIKE` does not); "starts with" could miss keys under some SQL Server collations; `COALESCE` runs its subquery twice on SQL Server and could lose a page; also any failure after the deadline was reported as a timeout, a refusal included, and no test showed the progress handler cleared. A decimal bound cast to double could miss a stored number by one unit in the last place (now parsed). **Mutation check:** 79 behaviours put back wrong (each filter and its negation, the key recheck and its padding, the prefix per database, the bounds, the ranges, states and tests, the walk, its first answer, keysets and ties, the cursor read-backs and their confinement, the tail, the one-pass count and its cap, the refusals, the budget's handler, deadline, cleanup, unwrapping and the timeout's classification); 78 caught. The fifth is equivalent: the `CROSS JOIN` that pins the values as the outer loop, which no statistics the tests could produce make SQLite need beside the unary plus; it stays as SQLite's documented order control (ADR-0039). Also: `scripts/ci-local.ps1` passed all six stages (288 s: 1,130 unit and 228 integration tests, 244 client tests, the browser projects 21 of 21); the naming check (`dotnet format style --diagnostics IDE1006`) clean; secret scan and check-text clean on every commit; name scan: in added lines only the four known false positives of the generic word in `MethodInfo` and `GetMethod`. **Gotchas:** SQLite's `LIKE` ignores `COLLATE` and folds ASCII case whatever the collation, so a binary recheck compares by code units only past ASCII (the keys are folded, so that is enough); SQLite carries an `e.id IN (…)` filter over to `v.entryId` through the join and then seeks the values by entry, which a unary plus on `v.entryId` stops; Microsoft.Data.Sqlite does not cancel a running statement, and SQLite keeps a progress handler on a pooled connection until it is cleared.
- 2026-10-24 · 4.3e The outbox repositories: action runs and emails · d5cae4c · **The outbox** (ADR-0040, internal `IActionRunRepository` and `IEmailRepository` over `jfbActionRun` and `jfbEmail`): every state change is one conditional statement that names the row and the state it must be in, and an attempt's outcome also the claim's token; a change that finds the row otherwise changes nothing and returns false, and no state change reads first. **Batches:** the runs one change queues for an entry are a batch (`AddAsync(entryId, formVersionId, changeKey, runs, wait)`; the submission, a confirmation, a user's, a rule's or an action's change; the key never empty and once per entry, `DuplicateKey` otherwise, checked after the entry's hold), inserted by position. An earlier run of the batch by `(seq, id)` holds a run back while it is running, waits for its first attempt, or stops the rest and is not final; batches of an entry are independent, so an uncertain stopper holds back only its own batch, and an approval's stopper never skips the submission's retrying runs. The due list (1 to 500, the longest due first) uses the claim's own condition, a locking read on SQL Server. **Claims** make the token (the claimant's name, a slash and 32 hex digits of new random bits, returned in `LockedBy`), so a late finisher never records over a later attempt, and write `Running` (`Sending`), the lease's end, one more attempt and the first start. **Outcomes** (succeeded or sent, again after a delay, failed for good, cut off as `Uncertain` keeping the token) only for the token's row while `Running` or `Uncertain` (the late finisher); a lease that ran out turns into `Uncertain` with `lease_expired`, keeping the token. **Stopping the rest:** a stopper's failure for good, also a late one, skips the later pending runs of its batch with `stopped` in the same unit; its retry from the log brings exactly those back, and each second statement names the stopper's state itself. **A retry from the log** from the state the user saw (`Failed`, `Uncertain`): due now, a new series with attempts at 0, the token cleared, `FormBuilderConcurrencyException` otherwise. **Undo** skips a change's runs that are pending without an attempt (`undone`). **Mail:** queued as it will be sent (addresses, culture and recipient key without control characters, attachments as a JSON array of file keys); every final or uncertain state sets the purge time and a retry clears it; the purge takes the earliest purge time first and names it again for the row it deletes (read-committed snapshot); deleting an entry's unsent mail holds the entry first. Errors are a code (`a-z0-9_.-`, at most 64) and a message cut to its column, never inside a surrogate pair; a mail stores `code: message`. The caller passes durations (wait, lease, delay, keep); the time comes from `FormBuilderDatabase.UtcNow`. Done when: `ActionRunRepositoryTests` (18) and `EmailRepositoryTests` (12) cover CRUD and every state change (claims once, ten at once, before due, a token per claim, the order within and across batches, every outcome, leases and the late finisher, stopping the rest and bringing it back, retries from the log, undo, the purge, deleting an entry), and a change with a stale token (one never issued, and another claim's live one) or a stale state changes nothing; `OutboxRepositoryArgumentTests` (10 tests, 18 with cases) show every refusal before the gate, the token's form, the cut and the stored error. **Review:** two subagent reviews against ADR-0007, ADR-0036, ADR-0038 and ADR-0040. The first found three bugs, all fixed before the first commit: the purge could delete a mail that a retry had just put back under read-committed snapshot; deleting an entry's unsent mail did not hold the entry, so a mail could be queued in between; a retry of a stopper brought back the stopped runs of other batches. It also found a contract gap (a reused token lets a late finisher record over a later attempt: the repository now makes it), the order and "stop the rest" reaching across the events of one entry (now batches, ADR-0040), and gaps in the tests and docs. The second found no bug and three gaps, all fixed: one batch per change and entry (now refused), the un-skip's entry scope for a bulk change, and another claim's live token. **Mutation check:** 130 behaviours put back wrong (each condition of both claims and due lists, the order and its batch, ties, every outcome's state, token and row, the late finisher, leases, stopping the rest and bringing it back with their scopes, the series a retry starts, undo, the purge's time, order and size, deleting unsent mail, the batch key, the waits, the tokens, error texts, attachments and every refusal); 129 caught. The first run caught 123 of 126: one mutant did not build (rewritten), one passed only in the run and failed when rerun alone, and one is equivalent: a stale failure skipping the rest, which the skip statement's own condition on the stopper's state already prevents (the C# check stays as a short cut). Also: `scripts/ci-local.ps1` passed all six stages (277 s: 1,150 unit and 258 integration tests, 244 client tests, the browser projects 21 of 21); the naming check (`dotnet format style --diagnostics IDE1006`) clean; secret scan and check-text clean on every commit; name scan 0 hits in added lines. **Roadmap:** §4.4's order and "stop the rest" now say "queued with it", that the actions of different events do not wait for each other, and that a retry from the log starts the attempts over (ADR-0040). **Gotchas:** the schema snapshot is frozen (ADR-0033), so `changeKey` stays nullable and the repository enforces the batch key; under read-committed snapshot only the target table of an `UPDATE` or `DELETE` is read as its latest committed version, so a subquery over the same table needs the condition again in the outer `WHERE` (or a locking read).
- 2026-10-24 · 4.3f The file store, the audit log and daily stats · 7f3acd3 · **The file store** (ADR-0041, public `IFormBuilderFileStore` and `FormBuilderFileId` in `Core.Files`): ids are 32 lowercase hex characters from 128 random bits, refused otherwise by every method; a save reads the stream from its position, writes `<id>.partial` as a new unbuffered file no reader opens, flushes it to the disk (`RandomAccess.FlushToDisk`, the one blocking call) and renames it without replacing anything, so a second save of an id, or a file that appears meanwhile, is an `IOException` and stays as it was; a failed, cut-off or cancelled save deletes only its own partial file, and one it cannot delete is logged without its path. Reads share the file for deleting; a delete renames `<id>` to `<id>.deleted-<random>` first, so the id reads as missing at once, and bytes it cannot delete are logged and left for 13.1's sweep (which now also takes `.deleted-` files). **The root** (`JironFormBuilder:Files:Root`, default `~/umbraco/Data/JironFormBuilder/Uploads`) is `~/` and a path relative to the content root, or an absolute path; device paths and stream names are refused on Windows; a root inside the web root or around it, as written or with junctions and symbolic links followed (at most 32), fails `ValidateOnStart`, so the site does not start; Web supplies the folders from `IWebHostEnvironment` (`IHostFolders`), since Umbraco's path mapping is obsolete in 17. **The audit log** (`IAuditRepository`, `AuditDetails`): details take keys, numbers, flags, defined enum values, UTC times and identifiers only; entries join the change's unit and come back as stored (SQL Server rounds times); `AddOnceAsync` is one `INSERT … WHERE NOT EXISTS` with a locking read, windows of 1 s to 24 h; queries filter by action, type, item, user and a time range within `DATETIME`'s years, newest first with the total; the purge deletes up to 1,000 oldest entries. **Daily stats** (`IDailyStatRepository`): a unit holds the form's row first (`FormRepository.HoldAsync`), as the form's delete will, then updates or inserts each merged count in ordinal order, so counts never reach a deleted form and never deadlock; counts of 0 to `int.MaxValue`, lists of up to 366 days; a form's values and retention go a form and a day at a time. Tests: the store in a site (14), the root with real junctions and symbolic links, every method and field of `AuditDetails`, no field value of an entry anywhere in the log (with the search tested both ways), counters under parallel increments and while the form is deleted; a mutation check broke 130 behaviours one at a time, and each made a test fail. From ADR-0044 (4.5a): **§4.8** sets the new-form defaults for every site at once (`[GlobalOnly]`), since a new form belongs to no site; the notify-the-creator default needs the email action and moves to **8.3**, with a Done-when check there; **5.1** declares the storage kinds that the field catalogue records from 4.5a on; **6.1** reads the published version through a cache with a form cache refresher, raised by publish, unpublish, rename, trash, restore and delete, and answers a trashed form as missing, with a Done-when check; ADR-0002 is amended (a save or a publish that changes nothing writes no version).

- 2026-10-24 · 4.5d · Trash, delete and folders (ADR-0047; ADR-0033, ADR-0036 and ADR-0044 amended): **The trash key:** `jfbFolder` and `jfbForm` gain `trashKey`. A form's trash gives it a key of its own; a folder's trash gives the folder, the live folders below it and the live forms in them one new key in one unit, with a walk that locks what it reads. A folder's restore brings back the folders below it that carry its key, reached only through such folders, and the forms with it, into its parent or to the root when the parent is in the trash. Nothing lives below a folder in the trash: creates and moves need a live parent, and a folder in the trash is neither moved nor renamed. **Forms:** the form service trashes, restores (into its folder, or to the root) and deletes, each at the concurrency version the caller read; the delete works only from the trash (`not_trashed`). It deletes the entries in batches of 100, each a unit that holds the form in the trash at that version, lists their files and deletes their unsent mail (sent mail stays without its entry); then the uploads that wait for an entry; then, in one unit, the form's rows, its grants, its references (the new `IReferenceRepository`), its daily statistics and `form.deleted` with the counts, going back up to three times for entries that came in late (`in_use` after that). The bytes go after each commit, and a failure is logged by the file's key without its path. **Folders:** the new internal `IFormBuilderFolderService` gets, lists, creates, renames (only the name, through the new `RenameAsync`), moves, trashes, restores, deletes (only from the trash, `folder_not_empty` while anything below it is outside it) and empties the trash. Each change has notifications before and after it, which for a tree name its folders nearest first and its forms by name, in the same order before and after, and `folder.*` audit entries. The names of forms and folders follow one rule (`LibraryNames`). **Checks:** 9 new unit tests (the notifications' arguments, the name rule) and 39 new integration tests (the repository's trash keys, holds and batches; each service operation with its refusals, notifications and audit entries, on a file store in memory). The Done-when checks are `FormTrashServiceTests.ADelete_RemovesTheFormWithEverythingOfIt_AndItsFilesFromTheFileStore` (the files leave the file store, and no reference or daily stat of the form is left) and `FormTrashServiceTests.AStaleSave_TrashRestoreOrDelete_ReturnsConcurrencyConflict_AndChangesNothing`. Writing the tests turned up two fixes: a change's notifications named the folders of a tree in two orders (nearest first before, by name after), and emptying the trash named its folders flat by name. A mutation check of 137 changes caught 131 at first: 4 did not build (rewritten), 1 ended after 3 s without running its tests (caught when run again), and 1 showed a gap (a restore kept the folders below in place on SQLite only through the order it writes rows in; a test with a folder older than its parent added). In the end all 137 were caught. Gate: all checks passed in 367 s (unit 1657 passed and 1 skipped, the symbolic-link test; integration 452; client 244; browser 23).

- 2026-10-25 · 4.6a · The permission model and its evaluation · ea94a41 · **The model** (ADR-0048, amending ADR-0013): the public `[Flags] FormBuilderPermissions` (design 1 to privacyTools 1024, never renumbered) and `PermissionRules`: names in any letter case, the seven that work in folders and forms and the four that are global only, what each needs (`viewSensitive`, `editEntries`, `deleteEntries` and `export` need `viewEntries`; `manageActions` needs `design`), and a stored bit this version does not know reads as nothing. **The evaluation** (`AccessSnapshot`): no section, nothing; an administrator (Umbraco's `admin` alias), everything; otherwise the union of the user's groups, globally, at the root (the folder-level part of the global grants), on a folder (its own, plus its parent's unless restricted) and on a form (its own, plus its folder's unless restricted), with an iterative walk that keeps each folder's result; an unknown folder gives only its own grants, and an unknown parent or a ring passes nothing on, so it fails closed. A move's gains per group leave out the item's own grants and the administrators, and a restricted item gains nothing (4.7 refuses such a move without `managePermissions`). **The cache:** one snapshot per server (every grant, the folder tree with the trash, the host grants resolved to group keys) in Umbraco's runtime cache for an hour behind a loading token; every committed change drops it once, here at once and elsewhere through `FormBuilderAccessCacheRefresher` (`RefreshAll`, id `5f0d6a2e-8c3b-4e9a-9a51-2d7c4be0f3a8`): grants, restrictions, folder changes (before their notifications, so handlers see them), permanent deletes of forms and folders (also one that stops part way, for what went), and user groups saved or deleted. **The filter:** a scope of folder ids as integer literals, the root and the groups with form grants of their own (a subquery on `jfbPermission`), one clause on `jfbForm` before paging and counting (`AccessSql`, `FormFilter.Access`); the folder list keeps the folders on the way to what the user may see, granted forms included (`FolderVisibility`). **Writes** (`IFormBuilderAccessService`): a grant from what the editor saw to the new permissions, refused for an unknown bit, a global-only permission on a folder or form, a missing dependency or an unknown group (all at once, on `access.permissions` and `access.group`), null for a missing folder or form, in one statement that reads the target's row with a lock, so a grant never outlives a delete at the same moment; restrictions of a folder (its flag only) and a form (through its row at the version read), outside the trash, a no-op writing nothing; audit entries `access.grantChanged`, `access.restricted`, `access.unrestricted` and `access.groupRemoved`. A user group Umbraco deletes takes its grants (a notification handler). **The host:** `JironFormBuilder:Access:Grants` (groups by alias) replaces the stored global grants and locks them (`409 setting_locked` naming the key), checked at start-up (missing group, a group granted again in any case, no permission, an unknown name, a missing dependency); `access` in `LockedSections` locks every grant and restriction. Done when: unit tests (`Tests.Unit/Access` and `AccessSqlTests`, 34) cover the evaluation with restrictions, the nearest boundary, unknown folders, a ring, dependencies, moves and administrators; `AccessServiceTests` (19) shows the cached evaluation following a grant, a restriction set and lifted, a folder created, moved in and out, restored to the root and deleted, a form deleted, a delete that stops part way and a user group deleted, each with one instruction to the other servers, the cache used until the refresher drops it and a raced load not kept; `AccessListTests` (23) lists forms (paged and counted) and folders as each persona of ADR-0013 over a library with a restricted folder granted to a separate group, a restricted form and a form in the trash; `HostGrantsBootTests` boots a site with host grants and its validator refuses invalid ones (with the unit tests that a site with them does not start); `AccessLockTests` locks the section. **Mutation check:** 137 behaviours put back wrong, all caught in the end. The first pass left one uncaught (a form grant that keeps a global-only bit: a test added), showed a dead branch (a restricted folder's inheritance, which the walk already stops: the branch went and the mutant moved to the walk) and had three mutants that did not build (rewritten); two were not made, a ring walked without its guard (it would only grow memory until it fails) and skipping the memo (only slower). A PC crash stopped the run at mutant 107 and left that mutant in the refresher; the run's target check found it, the line was restored and the run went on from there. **Also:** a permanent delete now drops the snapshot once per operation instead of once per form; ADR-0013 and ADR-0048, architecture.md (Core's Access area, the evaluation, host grants), database.md (the meaning of `jfbPermission`'s bits) and tests/README.md updated. Gate: `scripts/ci-local.ps1` passed all six stages in 393 s (unit 1,695 passed and 1 skipped, the symbolic-link test; integration 504; client 244; browser 23); the feat commit builds on its own with no warning; the naming check, check-text, the secret scan and the name scan clean on both commits.

- 2026-10-25 · 4.7a · The management API base, the library tree and folders · 9280ca1 · **The base** (ADR-0050, `Web/ManagementApi`): every controller derives from `FormBuilderManagementApiController` (`[MapToApi("jfb")]`, the backoffice's JSON options, Umbraco's `BackOfficeAccess`), internal and added to MVC by a feature provider, under `/umbraco/jfb/management/api/v1`. Every action carries exactly one declaration, `[FormBuilderPermission(...)]` or `[FormBuilderSection]`, globally or on the folder or form whose key a route value, a query value or a body property holds. `FormBuilderAccessFilter` (order -3000, before the validation `[ApiController]` adds) checks the Forms section (`403 no_section`), the migration state (`503 migrations_pending` with `Retry-After: 10`), a body that holds the key but cannot be read (`400`), the folder or form (`404 folder_not_found` or `form_not_found`, the same for one that is none and one the user does not see), each permission through its policy in the table's order (`403 forbidden` naming it; a section declaration on a folder asks the new policy `Jiron.FormBuilder.Policy.Visible`), and last the request (`400 invalid_request`, by path only); an action without one declaration, or whose key names nothing, is refused and logged. `FormBuilderExceptionFilter` answers the services' refusals through `FormBuilderErrorCodes` (now also `validation_failed`, `setting_locked` and `query_timeout`); every refusal is a `FormBuilderProblemResult` that writes itself as `application/problem+json`, because an object result from a filter went out as `application/json`. **The endpoints:** `GET tree/root` and `GET tree/children` (folders first, then forms by name, paged across both; each item with the user's permissions and `hasChildren` for what the user sees in the folder's own state of the trash), a folder's get, create (`201` with `Umb-Generated-Resource`), rename, move (`design` on the new parent too; a move that widens access needs `managePermissions` and then the confirmation `widenAccess`, otherwise `403` or `409 widens_access` with the gains), trash, restore and delete (`design` and `deleteEntries` on everything it removes, ADR-0013 amended), and `GET` and `DELETE trash` (its top items as the user sees them; emptying it removes only what the user may delete for good and counts what stayed). In Core: `IFormBuilderLibraryService`, the folder service's `GetRemovalAsync` and an `EmptyTrashAsync` that a filter decides, `FormFilter.OutsideFolderIds` and `ListFolderIdsWithFormsAsync`. **OpenAPI and the client:** the document `jfb` names its operations `<Controller><Action>` and Jiron Form Builder's schemas as Umbraco names its own; `docs/api/jfb.json` is its reviewed snapshot (11 operations), from which `@hey-api/openapi-ts` 0.99.0 generates `packages/backoffice/src/api`, one function per operation, configured at run time from the backoffice's own HTTP client (`src/hey-api.ts`), with every character outside ASCII written as its escape. **Done when:** `PermissionMatrixTests` (5) finds all 11 endpoints through the API explorer, checks each declaration against a table written by hand (the reviewed exception list empty) and calls each as the outsider (`403 no_section`), as a persona who sees the folder but lacks the permission (`403` naming it), as one who holds nothing on the restricted folder and with a key that names nothing (`404`), and the section-wide ones as a user without grants; `PendingMigrationsApiTests` shows `503 migrations_pending` with `Retry-After` from all 11 while the plan is behind and normal answers once it ran (`401` without a token first); the endpoint tests pass (`TreeApiTests` 13, `FolderApiTests` 10, `RefusalBodiesApiTests` 1, `OpenApiSnapshotTests` 2); the client is generated and committed, and `generate-client.test.mjs` generates it again from the snapshot and compares. Two stay, as neither can be seen here: SQLite reads an empty `IN ()` as empty (the guard is for SQL Server, where the CI job runs the same test), and MVC treats an exception with a result as handled (the unit test checks the flag). - 2026-10-25 · 4.8b · The repositories after the review · ee97307 · **Locks and budgets:** the audit log's filtered list and its count run under the query budget (`FormBuilderQueryBudget.Default`, the 10 s the entries list had alone) and answer `query_timeout`; the due list of action runs checks the earlier runs of a batch as the claim does but without lock hints, as it only proposes, while the claim keeps its locking read (ADR-0040 amended; `SqlGuardTests` lists the due list's check as unlocked on purpose, with the reason); a create of a form or of an option list, theme or email layout checks its alias and its key with one `NOT EXISTS` each instead of one `OR`, so each seeks its own unique index and locks only its own range on SQL Server. **Units of work:** a unit inside another keeps the outer unit's cancellation and deadline and adds its own caller's (one linked token, or the outer one when the inner caller passed none), so a read inside a budgeted query stops at its deadline; a write inside a read or a query is refused, as the read's scope would commit it and a read may run again; the migration state is read as a unit of work, behind the gate and again after a lock conflict, so a busy moment no longer answers "pending" for five seconds. **Safer statements:** the folder update that only tests called, and that wrote name, order and restriction blindly even in the trash, is gone; a trash delete whose last statement finds nothing throws `InUse` and rolls back instead of committing a form without its versions. **One home each:** `SqlArgs` numbers the arguments of every statement (nine copies before, the query builder's size check kept as its own) and holds the one limit of an `IN` list, `MaxListLength` (1,000), which replaces three constants; `RowHolds` holds rows for every repository (five copies before, two of them internal statics other repositories called on the concrete entry and form repositories); consent wordings have `IConsentTextRepository` (ADR-0038 amended); the reruns' waits are an `ImmutableArray`; docs/database.md says how a count is added as the code does it (the form's row held first, then an update or an insert per count). The bullet said "entry numbering", but the finding it came from was the daily counts' text; the bullet now names both, and the entry numbering text was checked against the code and is right. **Done when:** `AuditRepositoryTests.AQueryPastItsBudget_StopsWithQueryTimeout_AndRunsOnce`; `ActionRunSqlTests` (the due list without `UPDLOCK, HOLDLOCK`, the claim with them, the same check); `IndexPlanTests.ACreate_ChecksItsAliasAndItsKey_EachWithASearchOfItsOwnUniqueIndex` (forms and option lists: two searches, no `MULTI-INDEX OR`, no scan); `FormBuilderDatabaseTests` (the kept deadline, the outer token kept and linked for reads, queries and writes, cancellation by either caller, the refused write; 27 tests); `FormBuilderMigrationStateTests.IsPending_AfterALockConflict_ReadsTheStateAgain_InsteadOfAnsweringPending`; `FormRepositoryTests.ADeleteWhoseLastStatementFindsNothing_RollsBack_AndTheFormKeepsItsVersions`; the repository tests pass (integration 585). **Mutation check:** 32 behaviours put back wrong (each token path, the refusal and its flags, the audit budget and its query, both lock choices of the due list and the claim, the `OR` and each missing check of both creates, the rollback, the arguments' numbering and checks, the query builder's size check, the holds, the consent wording's insert-once, backstop and registration): all 32 caught at first. The reader handed out `List<T>`, and the value types their arrays. **Equality, decided once** (ADR-0034 amended): records compare their lists by their items in order and hash them alike, so two reads of one document are equal; comparing by reference and writing it down was the other choice, refused because a public record whose `Equals` calls two equal definitions different is a trap for every extension point. Nothing keyed a dictionary by a record, and the diff compares JSON, so nothing else changed. **Checks** (ADR-0035 amended): one-per-form types are counted per type (a set of aliases instead of one flag that every such type shared, so a captcha and another one-per-form type were refused together); `FieldMessageTexts` lists the field messages once, with their JSON names and kinds, for the save checks and the structural checks, which each had their own list. **Done when:** `FormExportFormatTests.AFileWithThousandsOfBadOptionLists_AnswersItsFirstHundredProblems` (5,000 lists answer the first 100 keys; a bad form key and 300 bad pages answer 100 together); `ModelListTests` (no list of the full fixture, its value types or its projection is an array or a collection, casts throw, a builder's list changes nothing afterwards; two reads are equal and hash alike, a change deep inside or another order is not equal, however the list was built); `StructureTests.EachOnePerFormType_IsCountedOnItsOwn`; `FieldTypeSupportTests.TheMessageList_HoldsEveryMessageOfTheRecord_EachWithItsOwnKind` (names against the source-generated metadata, every kind once, each entry reads its own message); the shared vectors in both runtimes, and the definition, schema and logic tests, pass. **Mutation check:** 14 behaviours put back wrong (the kept limit and its size, the record's and the projection's copies, the text, default and operand views, equality by items, by object and the hash, one flag for every one-per-form type, a message missing from the list, read from another property or of another kind): all caught. - 2026-10-25 · 4.8f · The definition model's cost and names · e171aa9 · **Cost** (ADR-0005 and ADR-0030 amended): rules compare numbers over the wire form's digits in both runtimes (`ExactDecimal.CompareText`, TypeScript `compareText`): the sign, the integer part's length and digits, then the fraction without its trailing zeros, so a rule over a 10,000-digit answer builds no `BigInteger` or `BigInt` and no key; before, every comparison parsed both sides into numbers, and the number case of `isOneOf` made a key of each operand. Measuring that showed a second cost: Core's cleaning made two copies of every value it cleaned (a `StringBuilder` and its string), so the five conditions allocated 1,167,512 bytes; it now copies a text only when it replaces something in it, and the same run allocates 4,512. A pattern's run keeps one set of buffers (the threads at a position, those that advance, the closure's stack; three `Int32Array`s in TypeScript) instead of new lists at every character, and a set's test is a loop without a closure. The rule item reader looks at a copy of the reader for `match` or `conditions` and reads the item with the model's own converter, so a group nested 30 deep costs about what one level costs; `JsonSerializer.Deserialize(ref reader)` first skipped over the whole value to find its end, once per level. **Names** (ADR-0034 and ADR-0035 amended; the JSON is unchanged, and the full fixture still reads and writes byte for byte): `RuleCondition.Operator` (`op`), `FieldOptions.OptionList` (`list`), `FormAvailabilitySettings.Access` (`members`), `EntryReferenceFormat.IncludeYear` (`year`), `FormPrivacySettings.IP` with `IPHandling` (`ip`; the schema's definition renamed with it); inside Core, `DefinitionChange.ElementKind` and `Kind` (renamed in two steps, so the compiler found every use of the old `Kind`), Logic's `LogicItem`, `LogicCondition`, `LogicGroup` and `LogicRule` apart from the definition model's names, `FormExportFormat.cs` after its class, and `FieldTypeTraits.OnePerForm`. **One home each:** `StrictText` holds the strict opening of both readers (UTF-8 within the limit, a lone surrogate refused, bytes valid throughout); the hash is `CanonicalJson.Hash`, beside the writer, and what publishing refuses is `StructuralPublishCheck.Check`, both out of the serializer; the writer reports a value it cannot carry as `invalid_value` and only System.Text.Json's own depth limit as `limit_exceeded`, because the converters now refuse with `ArgumentException` (every refusal was reported as too deep); a null culture is refused with `ArgumentException` as documented, not a `NullReferenceException`; the reader of what the site stored is built with the serializer, not on first use; Core's static tables are frozen sets and dictionaries and immutable arrays. **Done when:** `LogicCostTests` and `cost.test.ts` (five conditions over a 10,000-digit answer, one of them against 20 operands as long, in under 50 ms, and in Core allocating less than one copy of the answer; numbers compare as the values they are, and what is not in the wire form compares with nothing; in Core a pattern's run over 10,000 characters allocates less than three times what its code points take, and in TypeScript its time grows about linearly over ten times the answer, which old code passes too, so only Core shows the buffers kept); `DefinitionValueTests` (the writer's refusal as what it is, the null culture, the stored reader built once for 64 parallel callers, both readers refusing text and bytes alike, a group nested 30 deep in under four times one level); `StaticDataTests` (no static field of Core is writable or holds an array or a collection that changes); `TextSemanticsTests` (a text that cleaning leaves alone comes back as the same string; replacements land where they belong); the shared vectors in both runtimes and the definition, schema and logic tests pass. **Mutation check:** 30 behaviours put back wrong in Core (the sign, length, integer, fraction and negative order of `CompareText`, the sign of zero, trailing and leading zeros, an empty fraction; comparing numbers by a parsed value and by a key as text; new pattern buffers at each character and a closure in the set test; always copying in cleaning, a lost prefix and a pair read twice; nested groups scoped by `Deserialize`; the writer's refusal as a limit and a converter throwing `JsonException`; the null culture let through; bytes not checked and a surrogate replaced; the stored reader built per call; a static array and a writable static; publishing without the structural checks; an upper-case hash; the JSON names `op` and `members` dropped) and 4 in TypeScript (the sign, length, fraction and negative order of `compareText`): all caught. Two mutants of the first run did not build (a name taken twice, an unused parameter) and were fixed and run again; comparing by a parsed value passed the 50 ms budget alone, so the test gained the allocation bound, which found the cleaning cost. Gate: `scripts/ci-local.ps1` passed all six stages in 566 s (unit 1,820 passed and 1 skipped; integration 601; client 269; browser 25); the feat commit builds alone with 0 warnings; check-text, the secret scan and the name scan clean on both commits. **Roadmap:** 5.1 gains the longest answer a field type takes, and 6.1 its check before the rules and the validation run, with a Done-when check (an answer longer than its type takes fails at its field, and no rule or pattern runs over it), as 4.8f asks.

The tag `baseline/schema-v1` marks the commit that completes this step.

### Handover after Phase 4 (2026-10-25)
- **State:** Phase 4 is complete, and nothing is half-done.
  - **Schema v1 (4.1, 4.8a; ADR-0033):** the plan `Jiron.Umbraco.FormBuilder` with its three steps, 24 tables and the SQL Server DDL rendered without a server; step 1 frozen at the tag `baseline/schema-v1` (4.8i, ADR-0054), with the hash of its snapshots pinned.
  - **The definition model (4.2a, 4.2b, 4.8e, 4.8f, 4.8h; ADR-0034, ADR-0035):** the records, the strict reader and the canonical writer, the schema, upgraders, the save and structural checks, and the public projection.
  - **The repositories (4.3a to 4.3f, 4.8b; ADR-0036 to ADR-0041):** the database gate and units of work, the library and permissions, entries, the query builder with its budget, the outbox, the file store, the audit log and daily stats.
  - **Configuration (4.4a, 4.4b; ADR-0042, ADR-0043):** the host options with their start-up checks and the backoffice settings sections with their cached reader.
  - **The form services (4.5a to 4.5d, 4.8c, 4.8d; ADR-0044 to ADR-0047, ADR-0053):** create, save and publish, versions, copies, templates, import and export, the trash and folders.
  - **Access (4.6a, 4.6b; ADR-0048, ADR-0049)** and **the management API (4.7a to 4.7c; ADR-0050 to ADR-0052).**
- **The review** (4.8a to 4.8i): before the freeze, 4.8a read the snapshots once more; subagents then read the phase's code for SOLID, naming, async and load-balancing safety, and their findings were fixed in 4.8b to 4.8f (the repositories, the services, the form service in parts, and the definition model's limits, immutability, checks, cost and names). A second round read those fixes, and its findings were 4.8g and 4.8h; a third read the fixes of 4.8g and 4.8h in 4.8i (eight findings and a ninth fault met while checking them), and a fourth read 4.8i's own fixes (two findings, fixed). Each round's fixes were put back wrong in a mutation run, and every mutant was caught. On load balancing: the settings and access caches clear through cache refreshers on every server, no static state can change (`StaticDataTests`), and a shared load runs outside any caller's unit.
- **Wiring sweep:** a read-only subagent took the inventory from the code, and each item was checked for a product use and a test of its effect.
  - **Host options** (`JironFormBuilder`, ADR-0042): `Files:Root` (the physical store and its load-balancing check; `PhysicalFileStoreTests`, `FileStoreLoadBalancingCheckTests`), `Limits:Definitions` (the serializer and the save checks; `FormBuilderOptionsTests`, `HostOptionsBootTests`, `FormServiceLimitTests`, `RefusalTests`), `Access:Grants` (`HostGrantsBootTests`), `LockedSections` (the settings host rules and the access lock; `LockedSectionTests`, `AccessLockTests`), unknown keys and the invariant-globalization check refused at start-up (`FormBuilderOptionsTests`, `GlobalizationStartupCheckTests`, `refusals.spec.ts`), and the appsettings schema (`AppSettingsSchemaTests`). **Later:** `Designer:SettingLocks` is validated now and applied in 5.1 and 8.1; the sections `LockedSections` may lock before they exist (`email`, `protection`, `files`, `privacy`, `integrations`) come with their features (ADR-0042).
  - **Backoffice settings** (ADR-0043): `general.newForms.consent` (`FormCreation`; `FormServiceTests.ACreate_StartsWithTheConsentField_…`), `general.history.draftDays` and `draftCount` (`DraftVersionCleanup`; `DraftVersionCleanupTests`), the cached reader and its refresher (`SettingsServiceTests`). **Later:** `general.timeZone` is validated now and read in 6.1; the saves (`SaveAsync`, `SaveSiteAsync`) get their endpoints in 12.4a.
  - **Endpoints:** 29 management endpoints under `/umbraco/jfb/management/api/v1` (forms, folders, the trash, the tree, versions; ADR-0050 to ADR-0052), each with its API tests, the permission matrix (`PermissionMatrixTests`), `503 migrations_pending` on every one (`PendingMigrationsApiTests`), the refusal bodies (`RefusalBodiesApiTests`) and the OpenAPI snapshot. No public endpoint yet: the public API is 6.1.
  - **Notifications:** 33 published (18 for forms, 14 for folders and the trash, `SettingsSavedNotification`), each raised by its service and shown by the service tests, including a handler that cancels; they exist for extensions, so no product handler is expected. Handled: migrations executed, application started (the file store's check), and user groups saved and deleted (`FormBuilderUserGroupHandler`); `FormBuilderBootTests` checks every handler is registered. The access and settings cache refreshers are tested across two servers' copies.
  - **Extension points:** `IFormBuilderFileStore` (replaced by a site's own: `FileStoreRootTests`; today the permanent delete calls `DeleteAsync`, and `SaveAsync` and `OpenReadAsync` get their callers with uploads in 6.1 and their readers in 11.2 and 13.2), `IFormPublishCheck` (read at publish; 9.5b and 12.5 add checks), `ISettingsHostRule<T>` (read at every save; the restricted and clamped rules come in Phases 7 and 13), and the mechanisms with no real implementation yet but used and tested: section migrations, definition upgraders and retention parts.
  - **Internal services and repositories with their callers later:** the exchange service (9.2); the access service's grant and restriction changes (12.4a); `IFormBuilderFormService.GetByAliasAsync` (6.2, `<jfb-render form="alias|key">`); `IFormBuilderFolderService.ListAsync` with a visibility (9.2, the target of a move); the generated TypeScript client of the management API (9.1 registers the first manifests, 9.2 the library that calls it); the repositories of entries (create and events 6.1 and 11.2, the query builder 11.1, notes 11.2), consent texts (5.4), key-values (9.1, 12.5), action runs (8.1), emails (8.2), email layouts (12.2), references (6.7), daily stats (11.5) and the audit list (12.4b), each with its repository tests now.
  - **Authorization policies** (ADR-0049): `Section`, `Visible`, `design`, `deleteEntries` and `managePermissions` are applied now (`AccessPolicyTests`, `FormBuilderAuthorizationTests`, `PermissionMatrixTests`, `access.spec.ts`); `manageActions` is checked inline today. Their endpoints come later: `viewEntries` 11.1, `viewSensitive` and `editEntries` 11.2, `export` 11.4, `manageLibrary` 12.1 and 12.2, `manageSettings` 12.4a, `privacyTools` 13.2.
  - **Menu items, sections and trees:** none yet; the section alias and its grant to administrators (`GrantFormsSectionV1`, `MigrationPlanTests`) wait for 9.1, which registers the section.
  - **Moved to the tests:** emptying the whole trash without a filter, which only the tests called (the product empties what the user may delete, through the filtered call).
- **Next:** 5.1, the field type framework: the registry (a collection builder with `FormBuilderFieldTypeBase<TSettings>`) with settings schemas from attributes, which replaces `FieldTypeCatalog.BuiltIn` (the roadmap note of 4.2b) and declares each type's storage kind for the field catalogue (ADR-0044); it is also where `Designer:SettingLocks` is first applied.
- **Gotchas:**
  - The tools damage text: write edit scripts with the Write tool and build odd characters with `String.fromCharCode`. A double-quoted shell string ate the backslashes of a path again in 4.8i.
  - System.Text.Json's source generation: `JsonPropertyInfo.Set` throws for an init-only property (set it through `PropertyInfo`); `WhenWritingNull` leaves out a non-nullable null even with `RespectNullableAnnotations` (the writer's `RefuseMissing` modifier refuses it); a converter called straight keeps no `Path`.
  - `JsonElement.GetString` and `JsonProperty.Name` throw `InvalidOperationException` for an escaped lone surrogate, which the JSON reader takes: read through `JsonPaths.TryGetString` and `TryGetName`.
  - A pattern's weight: an alternation weighs the sum of its options.
  - `cd` into a subfolder moves the session's shell for good: use subshells, `git -C` or absolute paths.
  - The gate's staged scans read the index: stage before scanning.
  - A mutation run edits source files while it runs: no `--check`, edit or build of those files until it ends.
