- 2026-10-25 · 5.1e Changing types · c39611f · **The map** (ADR-0059): `FieldTypeChanges` works out from the registry which types a field may become and what each change keeps and loses, from the declarations and settings schemas alone, so a site's own types take part like the built-in ones. A field may change from one type to another when the two differ, both hold a value of their own and neither stores `none`: 26 of the 33 built-in types change into each of the other 25, and content (heading, paragraph, divider, image, captcha), the grid and the repeatable group, whose values live in rows or child fields, do not. **What a change says** (`FieldTypeChange`): the settings kept, narrowed and lost, compared by name, kind and item kind and then rule by rule (a required target, bounds with exclusive ones narrower than the same inclusive ones and compared exactly as decimals, lengths, counts, allowed values, choices that keep some or none of their values, nested objects that keep some or none of their settings, and any JSON, which the target checks itself, as narrowed); the target's required settings that the change does not carry, needed before the field publishes; the common properties the source supports and the target does not (`needsOptions` is a demand, so it is a flag of its own: the target needs options the source lacks); the error kinds whose custom messages go; the operators of conditions that go, all of them when the target has no rule kind, from `Rules.OperatorsFor(kind)` in ordinal order; whether the operators that stay compare as another kind; and whether older answers keep their columns (text and long text share them). **Why one map, served:** families of similar types would have kept a pasted text field from becoming a date or a dropdown (9.3b), and a preview endpoint per type would have been a round trip for a draft that lives in the browser; 9.4 now serves the map with the field types and applies it to the selected field (roadmap change above). **Checks:** `FieldTypeChangesTests` (94: which built-in types change into which, in the registry's order; declarations with and without a value of their own and with `none` storage, both ways; 25 built-in changes restated apart from the code with every part, which together hold every part both ways; the operators of every rule kind; 50 setting cases, one per rule and edge; the needed settings carried, bounded and lost; a site's own type through the registry; refusals and null). **Mutation check:** 37 behaviours put back wrong (the offered changes and the refusal, needed settings, properties and options, messages, operators with and without a target kind, comparing anew, the columns, and every rule by which a setting is kept, narrowed or lost, down to the direction and exclusiveness of a bound and a missing source's most), all 37 caught; the first run was stopped for the owner's .NET update and repeated whole afterwards. - 2026-10-26 · 5.1f Designer locks · fa64243 · **The locks** (ADR-0060): `FieldSettingLocks` resolves the host's `Designer:SettingLocks` of kind `field` against the registry, reading a lock's text by its setting's kind (true or false in any case, a whole number as digits, a localised text as the text of every culture, a list, an object or JSON as JSON text, anything else as it is) and holding it to the setting's kind and rules as a field's own value would be; a value settings leave out counts as none. `FieldSettingLockStartupCheck` stops the site on a lock whose type or setting the site lacks (compared exactly, with the spelling suggested for another letter case), whose value the setting refuses (saying what it takes, never the value) or that keeps a required setting without a value, and leaves invalid options and broken field types to their own checks. **Where they apply:** `GetPublishedAsync` answers the published version with the locked values (null for a missing, trashed or unpublished form), and every publish check runs over the draft as the site will run it; stored definitions, the designer's draft, versions and exports keep the editors' values, which come back when a lock is lifted. A draft save refuses a locked setting with a value of its own (`409 setting_locked`, its path and the lock's key) unless the stored draft held that value for the same field and type, compared as its kind compares, so a lock that comes later never stops a form from saving. `GET field-type` gives every setting its `lock`: read-only with its value, or hidden without it, which never leaves the server; the client is regenerated. **Why by kind and not JSON for every value:** configuration holds texts, and most locks hold texts that JSON would have to quote; a list or an object cannot be a configuration value at all. The roadmap gives 6.1 the locked published read and 9.4 the locked and hidden settings in the inspector (roadmap change above). **Checks:** `FieldSettingLocksTests` (55: 18 values accepted by kind and 25 refused with what each setting takes, types and settings the site lacks, other shapes and action locks left alone, a site's own type's wordings, start-up through the host with invalid options and a broken registry left to their checks, the definition the site runs with child fields, the same instance when nothing changes, and draft saves by kind, list item by item, stored value and document order); `FieldTypeModelsTests` (the lock marks, a hidden one without its value); `FieldSettingLockServiceTests` (5) and `FieldSettingLockApiTests` (2) on a real site. **Mutation check:** 77 behaviours put back wrong (resolution and its messages, the shape checks, reading every kind, what a refusal says a setting takes, applying, saving, comparing, start-up and its registration, the model, the endpoint, the service's save, publish and published read), all 77 caught; the first run left one survivor (the wording of allowed values, which no built-in setting has), killed by a site type's test. Comparing dates by value is not a mutant: `ParseDate` takes only `YYYY-MM-DD`, so the value and the text compare alike. Coverage showed three wording branches the settings reader can never reach (a list without a most, a text without one); they are gone, and the reachable ones are tested. Gate: `scripts/ci-local.ps1` passed all six stages in 532 s (unit 2,727 passed and 1 skipped; integration 625; client 311; browser 25); Core coverage 91.2% (22,742 of 24,942 lines; Web 99.2%), the locks, their start-up check, the model and the published read 100% of lines; the feat commit builds alone with 0 warnings and its 2,671 unit tests pass; check-text, the secret scan and the name scan clean (the term guard has no list, as before). Docs: ADR-0060, ADR-0008, ADR-0042 and ADR-0055 amended, `docs/architecture.md`, tests/README.md. **Gotchas:** a monitor that waits for a log file reports the previous run's log when that file is still there; remove it before arming.

Every requirement and check of 5.2 is in one of the two, the vector count now for the rules alone. - 2026-10-26 · 5.2a The rules engine · 6bd2b73 · **The engine** (ADR-0061): both runtimes evaluate a whole form, `Logic/FormRules.cs` in Core and `packages/logic/src/form.ts`, over one small model of the form (`LogicForm` with its pages, groups, fields, a repeatable group's child fields and a grid's rows, validation rules, actions and outcomes) that Core builds from a definition with `DefinitionLogic.From` and the vectors write directly. The whole form is checked before anything is evaluated (every condition, groups nested past 10 levels `too_deep`, effects an element does not take `invalid_rule`, a validation rule on no subject `unknown_field`, an id given twice or a repeatable group inside one `invalid_form`, a cycle `cycle`), in a fixed order, so the order of evaluation never changes an outcome; visibility goes in dependency order (Kahn's algorithm, ready elements in document order), a hidden element's answers are gone everywhere, and a repeatable group's child fields are decided row by row, seeing their own row there and every shown row from outside. The answer, `FormState`, lists what is hidden and required, the messages by field, each repeatable group's rows, the actions that run and the outcome. Publishing refuses a deeper group as `too_deep` at the group, with `Rules.MaxDepth`, the one limit both use; the publish checks and the adapter share one conversion of conditions. **Why a model of its own:** the browser will hold the public projection and Core the definition; with one model both runtimes run the same vectors, and each turns what it holds into it. **Why row by row:** "show the end date in a row whose kind is a range" needs the row's own answers; elsewhere a child field's answers are those of every row in which it is shown. **Vectors:** `rules.json` (459 cases from `suites/rules.mjs`): every operator against every kind (135 cases of the eight operators every kind takes, the text, ordered and checked operators on their kinds and refused on the others), empty and multiple answers, today in Zurich, New York and Tokyo, context conditions, answers compared with answers, nested groups at and past the limit, switched-off rules, and 117 whole forms; with spike and port, 681 cases of conditions, rules and forms (the scripts' tests hold it at 300 or more). **Mutation check:** `tests/vectors/mutants.mjs`, in the repository: 190 mutants (every operator flipped, the condition check, the text, decimal, date and pattern modules, `own()`, the instant parser, the time zone check, the instruction count, the form engine; in C# the Turkish i's, noncharacters, the folded blocks, the form engine and the depth), 185 caught and 5 written down as equivalent with their reasons (the full-width small letters in both runtimes, the dotless i in TypeScript, a relative date's digits and range). The first run left 24 survivors, killed by 25 new vectors (every folded block and the capital sharp s, an astral noncharacter and the end of the noncharacter block, a seventh count digit, results that drop trailing zeros, 61 digits, impossible dates and times, an offset of 24 hours, reversed ranges and a bracket in a class, `~w` and the line separator, starred groups at the instruction limit, a hidden child field in its own row, a validation rule that matches on a hidden field, cleaned answers in a form). Two C# mutants written down as equivalent were caught: .NET's invariant casing maps neither Turkish i, so Core's own case is needed; the notes are gone. Lone surrogates are the one input no vector can hold (.NET's JSON reader refuses them), so their mutants also run `text.test.ts` (new) and `TextSemanticsTests`. The scripts' tests run `mutants.mjs --check`, so a change that moves a mutant's code updates the script in the same commit. **Checks:** both runners pass all 723 vectors (727 tests in each); `StructureTests` refuse groups nested too deep in a field rule, a validation rule and an outcome at the group's path and accept them at the limit; `DefinitionLogicTests` (7) show the model of the structure tests' publishable document and what the engine decides from it with nothing and with everything answered. Gate: `scripts/ci-local.ps1` passed all six stages in 517 s (unit 3,207 passed and 1 skipped; integration 625; client 773; browser 25); Core coverage 91.3% (22,966 of 25,161 lines; Web 99.2%), the form engine 100% of lines (171), `DefinitionLogic.cs` 34 of 36 (the two left are the arms the compiler requires for kinds of rule items and operands that the closed model cannot have), `DefinitionStructureChecks.Rules.cs` 100% of lines; the feat commit builds alone with 0 warnings and its 2,731 unit tests pass; check-text, the secret scan and the name scan clean (the term guard has no list, as before). Docs: ADR-0061; ADR-0005, ADR-0030 and ADR-0035 amended; ROADMAP §4.3.3 (a whole form at once, the depth, what publishing refuses); `docs/architecture.md`; tests/README.md. Coverage found two dependencies no vector exercised, a field named inside a list operand of a show rule and one named inside a nested group of its conditions: four vectors and two mutants in each runtime now cover them, all four caught, and a guard no input reaches is gone. **Gotchas:** the first gate failed on `StaticDataTests`: the engine kept its empty answers in a static `Dictionary`, which the architecture test refuses as changeable; it is `FrozenDictionary.Empty` now. A later gate run, on a machine just restarted, failed 5.1c's `FieldTypeAvailabilityTests` after ten seconds: the availability service read its stop token inside the delegate it hands the thread pool, so a check the pool started only after the time limit found the token's source disposed, and reading it threw; the token is read before the check starts now (a fix commit of its own), and the class passed 15 runs in a row before the gate ran again. One rerun was void: started while the previous job's coverage run still tested, it found the test outputs locked; a chained job is done only when its last command is.

- 2026-10-26 · 5.2b The checks' contract and the text, number and date types · a64434f · **The contract** (ADR-0062): `FieldValidation` tells a type's check whether the field is required, by its own setting or a rule (`IsRequired`), and the site's date (`Today`), both handed over by `FieldValues.Check`; the public projection writes every public setting with its effective value, the field's own or else its type's default, an object's settings each in turn, so the browser checks what the server reads (the reviewed full projection grows by those defaults, and a value given equal to its default projects and hashes as none); an email's confirmation has a message kind of its own, `confirmation`, in the model, the schema and the field types endpoint (the snapshot reviewed). **The checks** of `text`, `textarea`, `email`, `url`, `number`, `slider`, `rating`, `scale`, `date`, `time` and `datetime`, in each type's `Validate` in Core (`BuiltInValidation` holds what they share) and in `checkField` (`packages/logic/src/checks.ts`): lengths in UTF-16 code units, words between the whitespace cleaning trims, exact ranges and steps from the minimum or 0 (a number without a step keeps its decimals' places), whole numbers for ratings and scales, inclusive date windows relative to the site's today (a bound outside the calendar, of another shape or with no offset lets no date through), weekdays from Monday to Friday and excluded dates, times to the second with a step from the earliest time or midnight, a date and time's step from midnight of its own day, a URL's scheme before its first colon in ASCII lowercase, and an email typed again compared folded, once. An email's domains stay on the server: the text after the last @, in IDNA's ASCII form, a listed domain or one below it. A limit or a step that is no number, or a step of 0, limits nothing in either runtime, so a draft's preview never fails on settings that publishing refuses. **Why the projection and not a table of defaults in TypeScript:** one source for the server, the browser and the headless renderer, and a site's own type gets it too. **Vectors:** `validation.json` (170 cases from `suites/validation.mjs`) through the new runnable `field` in both runners, which hold each case to what both runtimes see alike: every public setting with a default given, the type's kind, answers cleaned as the pipeline cleans them, and in Core readable and in the wire form. **Mutation check:** 62 mutants of the checks in TypeScript (`checks.ts`, `countWords`, `isMultiple`), 60 caught and 2 written down as equivalent (lowercasing a scheme fully, since only the Kelvin sign lowercases to an ASCII letter and no scheme a field allows holds a k; the guard that reports each kind once, which no check of these types needs before 5.2c); the first run left one survivor, a fraction whose digits are in range (`0.5` as a rating), killed by two vectors; all 208 TypeScript mutants pass. **Checks:** `BuiltInValidationTests` (107: each check of the eleven types at its edges through its `Validate`, drafts with malformed limits and a zero step, the confirmation in any letter case, missing or given twice, the domains allowed and blocked, below a listed domain, in capitals, in IDNA both ways and without a domain, the confirmation before the domain, and the types without checks of their own finding nothing even when required); `ProjectionTests` (the effective settings of nine fields, and a value equal to its default keeping the hash); `FieldValuesTests` (whether the field is required, as the rules leave it, and today reach the check); `FieldTypeSupportTests`, `FieldTypeChangesTests` (email to text loses the confirmation's message), the definition generator, and `FieldSettingLockServiceTests` (visitors get a locked field's other defaults too). Gate: `scripts/ci-local.ps1` passed all six stages in 515 s (unit 3,497 passed and 1 skipped; integration 625; client 944; browser 25); the feat commit builds alone with 0 warnings and its 3,207 unit tests pass (1 skipped); **Core coverage was not measured:** Smart App Control, enforced on this machine, refuses to load the copy of `Core.dll` that the coverage collector rewrites (Code Integrity event 3077, 0x800711C7, "did not meet the Enterprise signing level"), so both coverage runs found no tests; the gate loads the normal build. The machine's security settings stay as they are; the Phase 5 review measures coverage with instrumentation that rewrites no file; check-text, the secret scan and the name scan clean (the term guard has no list, as before). Docs: ADR-0062; ADR-0002, ADR-0055 and ADR-0058 amended; ROADMAP §4.3.3 (the `confirmation` kind); `docs/architecture.md`; tests/README.md. **The availability test** (a commit of its own): the first clone run of the feat lost one test, the time limit of a slow availability check, which 5.2a had made less likely but not gone. The test gave the check ten seconds after the limit to see its token cancelled, and a busy parallel run can keep the thread pool from starting the check that long; it now fires the limit once the check listens to its token, and every wait on the pool is a one-minute guard against a hang, never a measure of time; a new test shows that a check that looks at its token only after the limit still finds it cancelled; the clone's second run passed, and the class passed five runs beside the cost and projection tests. **Gotchas:** a vector first posted a text that NFC composes, which the pipeline cleans before any check, so both runners now refuse an answer that is not cleaned; the fixers re-wrapped a long line in `checks.ts`, so two mutants lost their code and the gate's first stage failed: run `node scripts/fix.mjs` before `mutants.mjs --check`, and keep mutated lines short.

- 2026-10-26 · roadmap change · 5.3 split in three (rule 10): planning it showed two languages that share nothing but the exact decimals and the calendar, and merge-tag sources and their interfaces that are a step of their own. **5.3a** builds the calculation language in both runtimes (the parser, the evaluator, the date functions and the limits), calculations in the form engine (ADR-0061) and their checks at publish, with `calculations.json`; **5.3b** the merge-tag language (the grammar, the filters, the escaping contexts, unknown tags, the limits, lists and the culture patterns) with `merge-tags.json` and the injection test; **5.3c** the sources with `entry.reference`, answer recall, the merge-tag interfaces and the copied actions' report. - 2026-10-26 · 5.3a Calculations · d597469 · **The language** (ADR-0063): `Calculations` in Core and `calc.ts` read a formula by one grammar (numbers in the wire form, fields by their bare alias, `+ - * /`, parentheses, one comparison per level giving 1 or 0, negations counted rather than nested) into a tree, check it against the form and work it out over the exact decimals of ADR-0005, with `min`, `max`, `round` (places a whole literal 0 to 20), `if`, `and` and `or` (worked out lazily, left to right), `not`, and `daysBetween` and `yearsBetween` over date and date-time fields and `today()` in the site's zone (a year from 29 February complete on 1 March, a later start negated). Number, choice and checkbox fields give numbers (several answers add up, none is 0); an unreadable answer, an empty or doubled date, a division by zero or a result past 60 digits leaves the calculation without a value. Every mistake has its code and the UTF-16 position where it starts; formulas take at most 1,000 characters and nest at most 20 levels, so parsing and evaluation stay linear. **Why comparisons are numbers and aliases bare:** one grammar instead of a second boolean one, as spreadsheets read it, and the names editors see in the designer, with a name before `(` always a function. **In the form engine:** fields carry their alias and calculation; every formula is checked before anything is evaluated, a calculation waits in the dependency order for the fields it names, and rules, other calculations and validation read its rounded result, never what was posted; `FormState` gains `calculations`, row by row too. **At publish:** `DefinitionLogic.From` maps aliases and calculations; the structural checks run the same check at `…settings.formula` against the fields the visitor's browser has (a server-set field is `invalid_reference`, a field of a type the site lacks waits for it), with the character in the message, and a formula's fields join the cycle graph, so a calculation that reads itself, two that read each other and one whose field a rule shows by its result are `cycle`; the setting takes 1,000 characters (ADR-0056). **Checks:** `calculations.json` (295 cases, both runtimes: the grammar and every mistake's position, the functions, each kind, the date functions across leap days and both daylight-saving changes, division and rounding at their edges, and whole forms with calculations read rounded by rules); 10,000 random formulas in each runtime (`FormulaFuzzTests`, `formulas.test.ts`: built from the grammar, broken by chance, over random answers; each a result or a `LogicError`, every outcome reached, the slowest 0.6 ms in C# and 0.4 ms in TypeScript of the 50 allowed, the fastest of three runs); `StructureTests` (15 refusals at their paths, 3 acceptances, the messages), `DefinitionLogicTests` (the mapping, settings that do not fit, the engine over a definition). **Mutation check:** 111 TypeScript mutants (the grammar, every mistake and where it starts, the limits, each function and comparison, what each kind of field gives, the dates, no value, the decimals the language reads, and the engines' calculations) and 7 C# ones where .NET differs (its character classes, comparers and number parsing), and the 5.2a mutants of the engine follow its new code. The first run left two survivors, a hidden calculation worked out anyway at the top and in a row, until two vectors showed that a hidden calculation never asks for the instant; one C# mutant is equivalent (a name that starts with a letter outside ASCII is empty, so the formula fails at that letter either way). Whole runs: 387 TypeScript mutants, 380 caught and 7 equivalent; 51 C#, 49 caught and 2 equivalent; none to fix. Gate: `scripts/ci-local.ps1` passed all six stages in 517 s (unit 4,124 passed and 1 skipped; integration 625; client 1,380; browser 25); Core coverage not measured (Smart App Control, as in 5.2b; the Phase 5 review measures it with non-rewriting instrumentation); the feat commit builds alone with 0 warnings and its 3,822 unit tests pass (1 skipped); check-text, the secret scan and the name scan clean (the term guard has no list, as before). Docs: ADR-0063; ADR-0005, ADR-0035, ADR-0056 and ADR-0061 amended; §4.3.3; the field types snapshot (the formula's 1,000 characters); `docs/architecture.md`; tests/README.md. **Gotchas:** the formatter re-wraps a line that grows past its width, so two mutants written against the unformatted engine lost their code: run `scripts/fix.mjs` before writing a mutant's code; a fuzz generator that picks every token alike reaches mistakes far more often than values, so it leans towards valid formulas and the test holds it to reaching every outcome.
- 2026-10-26 · 5.3b The merge-tag language · ddbf6f9 · **The engine** (ADR-0064): `MergeTags` and `CultureFormats` in Core, `template.ts` and `culture.ts` in `packages/logic`, read a template once for its context (`html`, `text`, `header`, `url`, `json`, `jsonString`, `form`, `xml`) into text, tags and tags written as their text, and render it over the sources' values: text, text with the wire number or date behind it, or lists of them. The filters `default`, `truncate`, `upper`, `lower`, `number`, `currency`, `date`, `join`, `url`, `json` and `html` combine with the context's encoder without a breakout (an explicit encoding is last, and only one), dates, numbers and currencies format with the culture's patterns (a subset of .NET's, written by 5.3c from the server), and `upper` and `lower` map case as folding does, one character to one in the folded blocks. Unknown tags render empty with a warning; malformed, misplaced or unclosed ones render as their text with an error, each problem at the UTF-16 position where it starts; 200 tags, 10 filters a tag, 100,000 code units of output; nothing throws. **Where a tag may stand:** each context scans the template's own text, never a value. HTML is read as its tokenizer reads it (a quote opens a value only after `=`; `</` before a non-letter is a comment to the next `>`; a comment ends at `>` right after `<!--`, at `-->` and at `--!>`; script, style, textarea, title, xmp, iframe, noembed and noframes end only at their own end tag), and where a browser's reading depends on the whole document (plaintext, `<!--` in a script, a raw element after svg, math, select or frameset, noscript, CDATA after svg or math) every later tag is refused; XML adds its comments, CDATA, instructions and DOCTYPE literals and refuses all after an internal subset; a URL takes a tag only after its path starts and only for `http` and `https`; JSON never right after a backslash in a string. **Why the scan refuses rather than guesses:** a wrong guess about where markup ends lets a value become an attribute or a script, while a refusal is a form-check error the editor sees at once. **Checks:** `merge-tags.json` (521 cases, both runtimes: the grammar and every malformed tag, every filter at its edges, each encoder, the places a tag may and may not stand, the explicit filters in each context, lists, eight cultures written as data (six real ones and two that test every pattern token and missing names), the limits and every problem); 10,000 random templates in each runtime (`TemplateFuzzTests`, `templates.test.ts`: every context and culture, every problem reached at least 20 times, the slowest 7.0 ms in C# and 7.7 ms in TypeScript of the 50 allowed, the fastest of three runs); the injection test (hostile values through every filter in every place add no element, attribute, header line, address part or JSON token, and the refused places render as written); lone surrogates become U+FFFD wherever the output must be well formed; the tables check gains `upper` and `lower` for every code point. **Mutation check:** the template module (295 TypeScript mutants), the culture formats (54) and the case mappings (34), and 12 C# ones where .NET differs. The first runs left 18 survivors: 9 in the template module and 1 in the culture formats were killed by seven new vectors and one culture pattern (the UTF-8 lengths at their edges, a surrogate pair through `json`, a filter name with digits, `default` of an empty list as one text, `</` before a digit, an XML declaration that starts with a quote, a tag cut at the limit after its own problem, day names beside a nominative month); 8 are equivalent, explicit case rules that one runtime's mappings already keep (TypeScript's for the dotted capital I and the letters with a ypogegrammeni, C#'s for the Turkish i's and the capitals of the ypogegrammeni range). Rechecking the equivalent mutants of earlier steps found `fold-full-width-small` caught in both runtimes now that `upper` reads the folded blocks, so its note is gone; the other seven still hold. ### Handover after Phase 5 (2026-10-28)
- **State:** Phase 5 is complete, and nothing is half-done.
  - **The field type framework (5.1a to 5.1f; ADR-0055 to ADR-0060):** the registry of the 33 built-in types and a site's own (`builder.JironFormBuilder().FieldTypes()`), declarations and their contradictions refused at start-up, settings schemas from attributes with their rules, each type's check of its own settings, the field types endpoint with availability, the answers contract (`AnswerLimits`, `Read`, `Validate`, rows), the change-type map and the designer locks.
  - **The logic engines in both runtimes (5.2a to 5.2c, 5.3a to 5.3g, 5.4a, 5.4c; ADR-0061 to ADR-0069, ADR-0071):** the rules engine, the built-in types' checks, calculations, the merge-tag language with its service, sources and filters (the answers, the Umbraco sources and the site's own), answer recall in visitor texts and copied actions, every one held to the shared vectors (D10) and the mutation check (5.2a).
  - **A submission's answers (5.4b, 5.4d; ADR-0070, ADR-0072):** storing answers and reading them back typed, and `SubmissionReader`, which reads, checks and resolves a post's answers for 6.1's pipeline.
  - **Option lists (5.5a to 5.5e; ADR-0073 to ADR-0078):** library lists and sources, the fixed, content, data type, URL and file kinds, choices held to their resolved options with their labels, and lists from an import; ADR-0074 made Umbraco 17.6 the floor.
  - **The review's fixes (5.5f to 5.5k; ADR-0079 to ADR-0083):** coverage without rewritten assemblies, a submission's answers, what visitors and failures cost the option lists, the ports of calls to other hosts, and merge tags and the two runtimes.
- **The review** (5.5f to 5.5k): four readers read the phase's code (the field types and a submission's answers; the option lists and calls to other hosts; the rules, calculations, merge tags and both runtimes; the wiring sweep) and found 19 things to fix, two of them high (`{{ field }}` without an alias threw in a visitor text, and a cascading field's posted parent answers became cache keys and reached sources unchecked), fixed in 5.5f to 5.5h. A second round read those fixes, one reader per fix commit, and found eleven things and seven missing tests, two of them high (an upload claimed by its token's text, so two tokens for one upload reached the entry's writer, and a hidden default's labels asked for the posted parent answers): 5.5i. A third read 5.5i's fixes and found no high finding but one medium (in merge tags, a repeatable group's child was asked for every row's parent answers at once, so two rows sharing a value showed one label), with four code fixes, nine doc corrections and eight missing tests: 5.5j. A fourth read 5.5j's fixes and found two medium findings: a grid whose columns come from a list or source showed its values instead of their labels; and a query value or default that a site's server-side type refused made the submission `invalid` at a field the visitor cannot see, so such a value is now checked as it is resolved and not taken when its type refuses it. Five more code fixes (the prefill's log only for a field prefilled from the query, a collection item whose constructor throws, a stream disposed as the time limit passes, a server-side type that stores files refused, and a server-side field's options not asked for again), three doc corrections and seven tests added or strengthened followed: 5.5k. Every finding's test failed on the code before its fix, and each round's changed code got mutants of its own, every one caught. On load balancing: nothing of Phase 5 keeps static mutable state (`StaticDataTests`); what a scope reads is kept per scope on one server and never shared; a library list's cached options are refreshed on every server by `FormBuilderOptionListCacheRefresher`; and every server must run the same .NET and ICU versions (ADR-0083).
- **Wiring sweep:** a read-only subagent took the inventory from the code (55a9d26..df38c1c), and each item was checked for a product use and a test of its effect.
  - **Host options** (`JironFormBuilder`, ADR-0042): `Webhooks:AllowedHosts` (`OutboundHttp`, the URL kind's check and `FormBuilderOptionsValidator.CheckAllowedHosts`; `OutboundHttpTests`, `OutboundHostsTests`, `UrlOptionListTests`, `FormImportListTests`; webhooks use it in 8.4), `Webhooks:AllowHttp` (`OutboundHttp`, the URL kind; `OutboundHttpTests`, `UrlOptionKindTests`), and `Designer:SettingLocks`, whose field half 5.1f wired (`FieldSettingLocks` in the form service's save and publish and in `GetPublishedAsync`; `FieldSettingLocksTests`, `FieldSettingLockServiceTests`, `FieldSettingLockApiTests`).
  - **Backoffice settings** (ADR-0043): `general.baseUrl` (`SiteBaseUrl.GetAsync` for the entry, page and site sources; `SiteBaseUrlTests`, `UmbracoMergeTagSourceTests`, `SettingsSectionJsonTests`). **Later:** its endpoint and screen are 12.4a.
  - **Endpoints:** `GET /umbraco/jfb/management/api/v1/field-type` (`FieldTypeController.List`, `fieldTypeList` in the client; `FieldTypeApiTests` with its snapshot, `SiteFieldTypeApiTests`, `FieldSettingLockApiTests`, `PermissionMatrixTests`, `FieldTypeModelsTests`); the screens that read it are 9.3b and 9.4. No public endpoint yet: 6.1.
  - **Notifications and cache refreshers:** `FormBuilderOptionListCacheRefresher` (`OptionListService.Changed`, raised by an import and the lists' changes; `OptionListSiteTests`) and `OptionSiteCacheHandler` for Umbraco's content, content type, data type, public access and language refreshers (`ContentOptionListTests`, `FormBuilderBootTests`). Phase 5 publishes no notification of its own.
  - **Extension points:** `builder.JironFormBuilder()` with `FieldTypes()`, `MergeTagSources()`, `MergeTagFilters()` and `OptionSources()`, each booted with a site's composers before and after the core one (`SiteFieldTypeTests`, `SiteMergeTagTests`, `SiteOptionSourceTests`); `IFormBuilderFieldType` and `FormBuilderFieldTypeBase<T>` (`CheckSettings` and the settings schema at publish, the projection and the endpoint, `CheckAvailabilityAsync` through `FieldTypeAvailabilityService`, `Display` through `MergeTagAnswerTexts`, `Read`, `Validate`, `AnswerLimits` and `ValidateRows` through `SubmissionReader`, `UploadLimit` and `CheckUpload` through `FieldUploads`); `[FormBuilderSetting]` and `[FormBuilderChoice]` (`FieldSettingsReader`; `FieldSettingsSchemaTests`, `FieldSettingRulesTests`); `IFormBuilderMergeTagSource` and `IFormBuilderMergeTagFilter` (`SiteMergeTagExtensions`, `MergeTagRegistry`, the start-up check; `SiteMergeTagExtensionsTests`); `IFormBuilderOptionSource` and the public standard sources (`OptionSourceRegistry`, the code kind, the start-up check; `OptionSourceRegistryTests`, `StandardOptionSourcesTests`, `SiteOptionSourceTests`); and the TypeScript package `@jfb/logic` (its vectors and the mutation check; the website runtime reads it in 6.4, the designer in 9.5a).
  - **Menu items:** none; the backoffice's English and German localization manifests (`bundle.manifests.test.ts`, `lang.test.ts`) wait for the section of 9.1.
  - **Services:** `IFormBuilderMergeTagService` (`Check` through `MergeTagFieldPaths` and the copied actions' report; `RenderAsync` through `SubmissionReader` and `ConsentWordings`), `IFormBuilderOptionService` (`GetAsync` through `MergeTagAnswerTexts` and `SubmissionReader`; `GetListAsync` for 12.1's preview), `IFormBuilderFormService.GetPublishedAsync` (6.1). Internal, with their callers in 6.1 and 6.2: `SubmissionReader.ReadAsync`, `ConsentWordings.ShownAsync`, `EntryAnswerStore.CreateAsync`, `FieldUploads`; and `FieldTypeChanges` (9.4) and `OptionListService`'s changes (12.1).
  - **Found and given to later steps (5.5j):** `IFormBuilderMergeTagService.Paths` had no step to call it, so 9.4 now builds the tag picker's paths endpoint (with the permission matrix and the client) and 10.2's email editor uses it; `EntryAnswerStore.ReadAsync` was named by no step, so 11.2's panel reads its values through it, with a test; the option lists' screens lacked the option kinds' schemas, the code sources' names and labels, the `#jfbOption*` texts and the source picker, now in 9.4 and 12.1 with their checks; and §4.9 lists `IFormBuilderOptionService`. The visitor's path through these waits for 6.1, 6.2 and 6.4, whose Done-when checks are its first proof.
- **Next:** 6.1, the public API and the submission pipeline: §4.6 at its base path with CORS, ProblemDetails, limits and the `jfb-public` OpenAPI document; the form definition, `validate`, `entries`, `receipt`, `options` and `uploads` endpoints; and the pipeline of §4.4 end to end, on `SubmissionReader`, `EntryAnswerStore` and `GetPublishedAsync`.
- **Gotchas:**
  - The tools damage text: write pairs files and edit scripts with the Write tool, build odd characters with `String.fromCharCode` or `(char)0x...`, and write vector texts with the suites' `x('{5c}...')`. Long bash heredocs with quotes break the shell.
  - .NET's JSON reader throws for a raw lone surrogate in the text itself, not only an escaped one: check `StrictStringConverter.IsWellFormed` before `JsonDocument.Parse` where a client's text is read.
  - The decoders differ: a corrupt gzip or zlib body throws `InvalidDataException`, a corrupt Brotli body `InvalidOperationException`, and a truncated body of any of the three throws nothing.
  - `UmbracoIntegrationTest.Setup` is not virtual and starts the host: a booted test that needs the site's own start-up validation wraps `IStartupValidator` in `CustomTestSetup`.
  - Smart App Control refuses rewritten assemblies, so coverage uses dynamic instrumentation (ADR-0079); wait for a whole gate or coverage chain before another build, since its testhost locks `tests/*/bin`.
  - A per-scope memo keyed by the scope's identity (`ConditionalWeakTable`) must key everything that can change between renderings, here the parent answers it was read for.
  - A grid's values carry their row's key (`FieldValue.Row`): keep them out of a child's parent answers, not out of the grid's own labels or checks.
  - The container lets a constructor's exception through as it is, so reading a site's collection catches every exception, not only `InvalidOperationException`.
  - A field that keeps its value on the server is checked as its value is resolved (ADR-0080); the submission's check only asks whether a required one has a value, and never resolves its options again.
- **The gate:** `ci-local.ps1` passed (580 s, above), with the full Playwright suite (the smoke project, 25 of 25) inside it; Core coverage 98.8% (17,448 of 17,668 lines; Web 99.0%, 699 of 706), over the 85% the gate needs; `secret-scan --tracked` clean (1,496 files) and `term-guard --tracked` clean (0 patterns: no term list here), plus the scan for the 20 product names that head the private study's sections over 1,030 tracked files and 310 commit messages: 0 hits (the same patterns find 40 lines in the study); no `[~]` step to retry; `.private/` holds only `research/feature-study.md`, unchanged since 2026-10-22, and no mapping request; the wiring sweep is above.
- **Open owner items added:** none.

- 2026-10-28 · roadmap change · 6.1 split in five (rule 10, nothing weakened): the public API has nothing to build on yet (no base path, CORS, limits, `jfb-public` document, published-form cache, places repository or unique-answer query), and 6.1 holds six endpoints, the whole submission pipeline, its concurrency tests and the upload spike's port, too much for one step and one gate. The parts follow what each needs: **6.1a The public API's frame and the form endpoint** (the base path, CORS, problem codes, time limits, the declarations and their test, the `jfb-public` snapshot, the published-form cache and its refresher, designer locks and the `ETag`, the site's time zone and the allowlist test); **6.1b Visitor texts and the options endpoint** (ADR-0068's recall data, ADR-0081); **6.1c Uploads** (ADR-0006, the 1.4b port, the host's file limits), before the pipeline, whose file checks need upload tokens; **6.1d The submission pipeline** (`validate`, `entries`, `receipt` and every check of 6.1 about a submission); **6.1e Places on options and unique answers** (the two concurrency tests and the replay once an option is full). Every sentence and check of 6.1 is in one of them; 6.1a also says the public answer carries the site's zone, and 6.1d keeps "every public endpoint" for `503 migrations_pending`. Added while building 6.1a, nothing weakened: 6.1a's body limits refuse a declared length above them, and any body for an endpoint that takes none, from 6.1a on (counting a body without a length stays with 6.1c and 6.1d); 6.1b gives each consent field the `jfbConsentText` hash of the wording it shows, with a check, since the submission compares it (ADR-0009); and 6.1d turns the form endpoint's `open` false at the entry limit, which 6.1a leaves to the submission's count (ADR-0084).

CORS for an allowed origin without credentials, a `GET` or `HEAD` from any other without CORS headers and `403 origin_denied` for its other methods and preflights; an `https` origin on the host of an `http` request counts as the site's own (a proxy that ends TLS); a visitor who left gets nothing, the time limit `504 timeout`, anything unexpected `500 server_error`, logged with the endpoint's name only. **Declarations:** each action's rate-limit policy, body limit and time limit as attributes that refuse values out of range; the filter answers `413` (connection closed on HTTP/1.x) for a declared length above the limit or any body to an action that takes none, runs the time limit on the site's `TimeProvider`, and answers `503 migrations_pending` with `Retry-After: 10`. **Document** `jfb-public`: `x-jfb-*`, the media types, the frame's refusals on every operation, no sign-in; THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES lists all three), a lone surrogate as U+FFFD. **Published forms** per server with one load per miss, a not-served mark for forms that exist, nothing for names of no form (`RuntimeCacheLoads` takes a `keep`), an unreadable published version logged once per load; the form and folder services raise the refresher. **Time zone:** UTC for a stored zone this server cannot resolve, warned about once an hour per site and zone within a bound of 256. **Docs:** architecture §1 and §5, tests/README. - 2026-10-28 · 6.1d Uploads · 3dc25a0 · ADR-0087 written; ADR-0006, ADR-0071 and ADR-0084 amended (5ec9eaf). **The endpoint:** `POST forms/{key}/uploads?fieldKey=` takes one file, as `multipart/form-data`, for a field whose type stores files (a repeatable group's field and a signature among them) of every published form not in the trash: an optional `challenge` part first (held to 4 KB, verified from 7.1), then one `file`; the public API's no-form feature keeps everything before it from reading the body. **Limits:** the field's limit (its type's or `Files:MaxFileBytes`, whichever is smaller) plus 64 KiB for the request: a declared length past it answers `413` before a byte is read, the server's own limit is set to it, and the body is counted as it is read, so a chunked one is cut off there; the frame declares the largest upload (`FormBuilderBodyLimitAttribute.LargestUpload`) and sets the server limit of every endpoint with a body to its declaration. **Checks** on the first 512 bytes, before any is stored: the name cleaned (last path segment, no control characters, NFC, 255 characters with the extension), programs and scripts by their bytes and the site's blocked extensions, the type's own check, then the table of kinds (`FileKinds`), each refusal `415` with the field's kind of error (`fileType`, or `invalid` for a type that does not declare it). **Storing** (`FileUploads`): the bytes under a random id through a stream that counts and hashes them, then a `jfbEntryFile` row without an entry once nothing follows the file; whatever is not kept is deleted again, and bytes that cannot be are logged without the name. **The answer:** `{token, name, size, contentType}`, the token protected under its own purpose for 24 hours; every refusal closes the connection. **The host's limits:** `Files:MaxFileBytes` (50 MiB unless set) and `Files:BlockedExtensions` (a site's own list replaces the defaults), checked at start; publishing refuses a file field above the limit or listing a blocked extension at its path (`FieldSettingsCheck.Host`). **Roadmap:** 6.1e checks the form's availability for uploads too (line above). **Tests:** `PublicFormUploadTests` ports the 1.4b upload test to a real site with a 60 MiB limit, a middleware that counts what the server reads, and a watch of a buffer folder of the run's own (`ASPNETCORE_TEMP`): a 60 MiB file to a 50 MiB field cut off after 52,428,902 bytes read, 4.9 MB allocated in the whole process and the client stopped at 50 MiB, with nothing buffered, stored or recorded; one of exactly 50 MiB stored; declared lengths past a field's limit and past the largest upload refused with nothing read; renamed 20 MiB programs, with their length and chunked, refused on their first bytes; 23 requests it cannot take refused with their codes; publishing held to the site's limits. Tamper-checked: without the executable check, the check of what follows the file, the field's own limit, the declared-length check or the closing of refusals, 1 to 4 of its tests fail. `PublicApiPendingMigrationsTests` sends the upload a file and finds the store unchanged; `UploadRequestsTests` covers what a test server does not reach (a writable server limit, a form gone before the file is stored, the server's own 413); unit tests for the kinds, the stream, the storing, the tokens and the host's limits. Done-when checks run this session: gate `scripts/ci-local.ps1` passed in 609 s on the final tree (unit 6,300 and 1 skipped, integration 702, client 2,163 and the browser projects in Edge); a first run in 511 s failed on 3 unit tests (byte arrays held in statics, and the Files module's registration needing other modules), both fixed, and a second in 618 s passed before the last unit tests; Core coverage 98.8% (18,385 of 18,604 lines), Web 99.4% (1,233 of 1,241), every line 6.1d added or changed covered; the `jfb-public` snapshot holds `FormUpload` with its multipart body and `FileUploadResponseModel`; the feature commit 5ec9eaf built alone in a clean clone with 0 warnings and passed its 6,160 unit tests (1 skipped), the mutation check (1033) and the vector check; secret scan, text check, audit suppressions, notices and name scan (0 hits) clean, the term guard has no list, as before. **Gotchas:** the test server offers no writable server body limit, and Umbraco's front end answers a test site's unknown addresses with a page it lacks; `ContentDispositionHeaderValue.FileName` is decoded already, and unescaping it again loses a Windows path's backslashes; a literal `0` converts to any enum, so the largest upload is a string constant; a filtered unit run misses the architecture tests, so run them all before the gate.

- 2026-10-28 · 6.1f The validate and receipt endpoints · c3d439a · ADR-0089 written; ADR-0012 extended, ADR-0088 extended and amended (0ee82e7, 0c2c75b). **Validate:** `POST forms/{key}/validate` (operation `FormValidate`, policy `submit`, 1 MiB counted as it is read, 30 s) takes a submission's body with `pageKey` and without `submissionId` and `captchaToken` (`consents` joins section 4.6's list), reads, rules and checks every answer given so far as the submission does (the earlier pages' and a later page's a visitor came back from), and answers `204` with `no-store` when the page passes, or `422 validation_failed` with the errors of that page's fields only, by alias, a repeatable group's children and grid rows among them, the page found by its key in the revision the visitor's page showed and in the live version (an older revision's error at a field it lacked `409 form_changed`). It meets the submission's steps before the unit (the form with its row read again, members only `403 member_required`, the revision and the consent wordings `409 form_changed`, the times and the entry limit `403`), but idempotency and the empty submission; a page key that names no page is `400 invalid_request` at `pageKey` for the current revision and `form_changed` for an older one; `uniquePerForm` is left out, as only the submission's unit runs it (6.1g); nothing is stored, counted or claimed. **Receipt:** `POST forms/{key}/receipt` (operation `FormReceipt`, policy `submit`, 16 KiB, 30 s) takes `{submissionId, challenge}` and answers the receipt and the outcome of the form's entry with that id as a retry of its answers gets them, also once the form is full and for an entry in Spam or rejected; every miss (no such entry, another form's, a draft) is the same `404 not_found`, body for body, while a form the site does not serve answers `404 form_not_found` and a form for members `403 member_required`, the form's own codes, which depend on no submission id; the challenge is read and bound in 7.1. **In the pipeline:** `FormSubmissions.ValidateAsync` and `ReceiptAsync` share `SubmitAsync`'s steps (`ServedAsync`, the gate, `ReadAsync`), `SubmissionResult.Valid()` and the refusal `EntryNotFound`; - 2026-10-28 · 6.1g Places on options · 8cc251b · ADR-0090 written; section 4.4, section 4.5 and ADR-0088 amended, ADR-0012 and ADR-0033 extended (f504c1f). **What a place is:** an entry holds places while it counts toward the entry limit (unverified, submitted or approved, and no test entry); a place is one of its values at the field that equals the option's value exactly, each repeater row and each choice of several counting once. **The counts:** `jfbOptionCapacity.taken` is kept equal to that number. A row is made when an option first needs one (`INSERT … SELECT … WHERE NOT EXISTS`) holding the places held then, so a limit set after entries came in counts them; every write of the entry repository keeps it in the same unit (a new entry, a state change into or out of the holding states, a change of values, a delete of one and of several), each after an update of the form's row as its first statement, so a form's places change one unit at a time; the repository never refuses an overbooking, which is the caller's to decide (11.2 asks first). New: `IEntryRepository.GetPlacesTakenAsync` (reads only, counting the values for an option without a row) and `CountPlacesAsync`, `OptionPlace`, `OptionValueHash` (a lone surrogate refused as `ArgumentException`). **The pipeline:** the reader lists the choices of options with places whose answers stand (`SubmissionAnswers.Places`, a field's own items or the places a list or source gives); `OptionPlaces` holds them against the counts while the answers are read, for submissions and for `validate`, and again inside the submission's transaction once its own values are counted, where a shortfall throws `SubmissionInvalidException`, rolls the unit back (entry and number with it) and answers `422 validation_failed`. The first choices in the order of the form and its rows get the places left; a field fails once per row with `full`; the `full` errors join the reader's in the order of the form (a stable sort by field, row and child, a grid's rows with their grid). A retry is answered before reading, so it never meets `full`; `validate` answers `full` and makes no count. **Found by the checks:** the gate's SQL guard rule flagged the new count's check that the form exists as a read without the SQL Server lock hint (fixed before the commit); the first tamper run left three changes alive, all test gaps (an edit in the repository test had moved the value a single delete of a Spam entry was meant to keep, no edit moved a value onto a counted option, and no submission's second choice was the full one), fixed with cases of their own; rows and choices; the form's row locked first by a state change, an edit and both deletes; refusals), unit `OptionValueHashTests` 5. **Tamper checks:** 40 changes put back wrong, each with its own build: each write's count left out, turned the wrong way, applied to entries that hold none or taken without the lock; a count made from nothing, made twice or for a missing form; another field's, another form's, a test entry's, every state's and another letter case's values counted; option values past 200 characters or without a field; a lone surrogate passing as another exception; the early check, the order of the errors, row-major ranking, grid rows' rank, the transaction's check and its refusal, the unit's own values, the first choices' places, one error per field, places for a failing answer, a list's places, a group's rows and every choice: all 40 caught (37 in the first run; the three survivors above). **Gate:** `scripts/ci-local.ps1` passed all six stages in 647 s (unit 6,378 passed and 1 skipped; integration 754; client 2,168; browser). Coverage, run again on the final tree: Core 98.9%, Web 99.3%; every changed line covered. The work commit builds without a warning and passes the unit tests alone in a clean clone. - 2026-10-28 · 6.2a Website assets and `<jfb-assets>` · e2f6b23 · ADR-0092 written; ADR-0004 and ADR-0026 extended (d2f4cd9). **Serving:** a middleware before Umbraco's routing (pipeline filter `Jiron.FormBuilder.WebsiteAssets`) owns `/jfb/assets/` below the path base in any casing, as the public API owns its base path, and never hands such a request on, also while the plan is pending; only the originals the assembly holds are names, compared by code units (no case folding, `%2F` kept, no path of its own making, copies not names), anything else `404` with no body, another method `405` with `Allow`. **The copy:** Brotli, then gzip, then the original, as `Accept-Encoding` accepts with a quality above zero (`*` for the codings not named), each with its own strong `ETag` and `304` on `If-None-Match` (weak, listed or `*`). **Caching:** the fingerprint is the first 12 hex digits of the original's SHA-256; exactly one current `v`, or a name in a `chunks/` or `assets/` folder (named by content, which a module's `import()` needs), gets a year's `immutable`, anything else `no-cache`. Every answer `nosniff`; files `Content-Type` with UTF-8 for text, `Vary: Accept-Encoding`, CORS `*` without credentials and `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: cross-origin`. Read from the assembly once. **`<jfb-assets>`** writes the runtime's module script under the path base and the fingerprint, once per request (a request item; 6.2b's `<jfb-render>` shares it), never its element or children, with the nonce of its attribute, else of the site's `IFormBuilderCspNonceProvider` (new public extension point), else none; ADR-0092 notes that Razor runs a view before its layout, for 6.2b to settle. **Checks:** 100 unit tests (`Rendering/`: the catalogue with spellings, copies, types, fingerprints and every `Accept-Encoding` case; the middleware's answers and headers; the tag's once, nonces and children; the registrations made twice, and only the prefix's resources read, against Umbraco.Core's 52) and 16 TestServer tests (every built asset in the assembly with both copies, which decompress to it, served byte for byte with every header; unknown, cased, copy, `%2F` and raw traversing paths `404` without a page; the tag's address served for a year; the composer's filter once). Tamper 54 of 54 caught (two mutants first rewritten to build, one equivalent until the prefix test). Gate passed in 667 s (unit 6,500 and 1 skipped, integration 781, client 2,168, browser 25); coverage Core 98.9%, Web 99.4%, every new line covered; clean clones of d2f4cd9 and e2f6b23 built with 0 warnings and passed the unit tests; scans clean. No visible UI in this step: the Edge look comes with 6.2b's rendered forms.

- 2026-10-29 · 6.2i Rows and moves without JavaScript · 323f224 · ADR-0101 written; ADR-0096, ADR-0098, ADR-0100 and the architecture point to it (0695d27, 31407f9). **The buttons:** `FormPostHandler` reads `add:<alias>`, `remove:<alias>:<row>`, `up:<name>:<n>` and `down:<name>:<n>` without a check and stores nothing: Add starts a row as a first render does (its defaults, a ranking in its own order) while the group has fewer rows than its `maxRows`, Remove takes the row out while it has more than its `minRows` and the rows below move up with their answers, consents' wordings and rankings' orders, and a move swaps the option with its neighbour; a group or ranking is found by its alias now or one it had, a ranking in a row by its row's path. Each ranking now posts `jfb-order-<name>` once per option in the order shown; the move acts on it (the posted positions first), and the page shows the options below the top in it. An answer holding nothing but an order is no answer for the pipeline. **Where the page opens:** each of these buttons carries a `formaction` with its own fragment, made from the form's action: Add at the row added, Remove at the group, a move at the option's new place (`<field id>-place-<n>` on each option); Back, Next and Send open at the form as before. **Roadmap:** 6.3 strengthened (rule 10, nothing weakened): every theme gives those targets a block-start scroll margin, and its "Done when" checks at 375 px that a move opens with the option before in view, since the browser puts the target at the window's top edge. **Checks:** 17 TestServer post tests (5 new: Add and Remove at both limits with every answer kept and a row added starting with its default, moves past both ends and into and out of the top two with the stored ranking, a row's ranking that keeps its order when its row moves up, a move that follows the posted positions and one without an order, 21 buttons that name nothing) and 7 new unit tests (the reader's buttons, `RankingOrder`); the `website` Playwright project (Edge, JavaScript off) adds and removes a reference on the careers page and moves the feedback ranking into its top three, stored as left, checking where each press opens. Looked at in Edge without scripts at 1280 and 375 px: every state axe-clean, no horizontal scroll, no foreign request; a phone lands at the moved option. Tamper: 38 of 38 caught, after four test cases were added (a Remove one past the last row, an order alone outside rows, a ranking ranked again dropping a position, a rule on a moved ranking reading its top two alone) and three changes rewritten to build. **Found on the way:** a post without the order (a site's own view) first cleared the visitor's ranking on a move; it now moves among the ranked options. `member` is a reserved alias, so the tests' group is `team`. Gate: ci-local passes in 698 s (6,878 unit and 845 integration tests, 2,454 client tests, the browser projects in Edge, 30 tests). Coverage: Core 98.9%, Web 99.2%; every new line covered, the one Web line left being 6.2h's defensive null. Each work commit builds and passes the unit tests alone in a clean clone.
- 2026-10-29 · roadmap change · 6.2j split in two (rule 10, nothing weakened): its two parts each need a design of their own. Files need ADR-0006's marker, the limits middleware before routing with its drain allowance, the upload's rules in the surface action, the message for a post that is too large and the signed token that keeps a checked file across a re-render. The typed signature must become the signature's PNG on the server, and no image library may be added (CLAUDE.md rule 4: no direct ImageSharp, and System.Drawing is Windows-only), so the server needs a text renderer of its own and an answer for names in scripts it cannot draw. **6.2j No-JS posts with files** keeps the files and their "Done when" word for word; **6.2k A signature typed without JavaScript** takes the signature and its "Done when" word for word, and adds that an ADR decides how a name in any script is drawn and what is stored when it cannot be (added); the cache rules move one letter on, to 6.2l. The references moved with them: ADR-0095, ADR-0096, ADR-0097 and ADR-0100.
- 2026-10-29 · 6.2j No-JS posts with files · 05961ac · ADR-0102 written; ADR-0006, ADR-0087, ADR-0100, the architecture and the copy deck point to it (69cd294, a579804). **The marker and the reader:** a form whose definition stores files posts to `<page>?jfb-post=<key>.<instance>#<instance>`. `FormPostFilesMiddleware`, an Umbraco pipeline filter before routing, takes a multipart `POST` whose marker names a published form and an instance id as the render gives them, sets Kestrel's cap to the drain allowance (twice the form's limit: each file field's limit times its files and its group's rows, plus 1 MB) and reads the post part by part, at most 1,024 parts, 8 headers and 4 KB of headers each, a value up to 1 MB. A file part named by a field that stores files is buffered up to its field's limit (64 KB in memory, then a temporary file), at most as many as the field takes; past either it is noted at the field and the next part drains it; a part of no such field is skipped; an empty file name is no file. Past the allowance (a declared length is not read at all) or the reader's limits, the post goes on with no form and `no-store`, and the page shows "Your answers weren't sent" with the `too_large` text. **The surface action:** each file goes through `FileUploads.StoreAsync`, the upload endpoint's rules; a stored file's token joins its field's answer (replacing a kept one in a field of one file), and the request now carries the token reader, so the pipeline reads the tokens as it reads the runtime's. A noted or refused file keeps Back, Next and Send on the posted page with its error at the field beside what checking the page finds, while a row's or a ranking's button still acts. **The page:** each token that names a stored file of this form and field that no entry holds comes back as a ticked box above the file input, "Keep cv.pdf (97.7 KB)" / "cv.pdf (97,7 KB) behalten" (1,024 based, KB until it rounds to a megabyte, one place only when not whole, a few bytes as 0.1 KB); unticked, the file is let go; a row's files move with their row. - 2026-10-30 · roadmap change · 6.4a split in two (rule 10, nothing weakened): planning it showed that the browser has no reading of its own. Core holds every answer to its type's limits and reads it (numbers converted to the wire form, dates that must parse, one value where the type takes one, choices with "Other", parts, a grid, a ranking, yes or no, a consent, a country) before the rules and the checks see it; the shared logic starts at the checks, so `abc` in a decimal field passes in the browser and is `invalid` on the server. The form endpoint also lacks what the browser needs to decide as the server does: each field's kinds, whether it can be required and whether its type is the built-in one, and, unless a text holds merge tags, the culture's formats and the words for yes and no that the messages print with. That is shared logic in both runtimes with a vector suite of its own, a step apart from the runtime's DOM. **6.4a Reading answers in both runtimes** takes the reading stage with `tests/vectors/reading.json` and its mutants, the endpoint's kinds, formats and words, the error summary after a question (built in f64e751) and the messages vectors; **6.4b The runtime's frame, validation and rules** keeps the frame, reading the page's controls, validation, rules, the messages on the page and their delivery. The later parts move one letter on (6.4c to 6.4j), and the references follow (the Ligata port row, 6.2g's messages, 14.2's languages, ADR-0106). Added where they are built, each with a check: a cascading field's options from the `options` endpoint with the page's content key (ADR-0085, ADR-0086) in 6.4i, and a default value with merge tags filled in the browser (ADR-0093) in 6.4c.

Once the document is parsed, and for any root a site passes to `JironFormBuilder.init(root)` (the root itself when it is a form), it takes every `form[data-jfb-form]` of contract version 1, reads its model data (checking its shape: the alias, the fields' logic, the consents, the pages, groups and fields with their keys and types), enhances it once and dispatches `jfb:ready` on it with `{ key, alias }`; a form of another version, without model data or with model data of another shape, and an outcome or a notice in a form's place, are left to the post without JavaScript, and a second copy of the runtime enhances through the first's global. **The chunk** (`chunks/form-<hash>.js`, 6.28 KB gzip) loads on a form's first `focusin`, `pointerdown`, `input` or `change` (autofill changes a field without focus), once for the page, and is given each form once; when it cannot load, the forms stay with the no-JS post. The measurement that made it a chunk: the logic that validation and rules need bundles to about 15.9 KB gzip by itself, before calculations. **Reading the page:** `entriesOf` is `new FormData(form)` with its texts (files, disabled controls and buttons out, as a post leaves them); `readPost` (`packages/logic/src/posts.ts`) is the twin of Web's `FormPostReader` (names and parts, rows by `jfb-rows-<alias>`'s last value or as far as a name goes, rows and counts in ASCII digits as .NET reads them with trailing NULs and at most 100, consents' wordings, rankings' orders naming their rows, context and query values, switches left off as `no` in each shown row, a signature's typed name left out, CRLF as LF in a field's texts and a consent's wording only); then each field as Core's `SubmissionReader` reads a submission (a group's rows only while their number keeps its limits, the `hidden` type never taken from the page unless prefilled from the query, a built-in type through 6.4a's `readField`, a site's own type's cleaned texts), into the rules' answers by field, grid row and group row. `BUILT_IN_TYPES` gains `childFields` and `serverOnly`, held to the served `features` and `serverOnlyDefault`, and `MOST_ROWS` to the most a group's `maxRows` takes. **Checks:** `tests/vectors/posts.json` (85 cases) passes in NUnit (`PostVector` through `FormPostReader`) and Vitest (`readPost`); `posts.test.ts` holds what only the browser decides (a site's own type under a built-in alias, a site's own group, names of an object's own); the mutation check catches all 24 TypeScript and 11 C# mutants of the two readers (two rewritten: one that lifted the cap on a row count made a count of 2,147,483,647 run out of memory instead of failing a test, so it caps one row too high; one that left the line-break fields unread stopped the build on IDE0052, so it breaks the CRLF itself); Vitest in jsdom covers the frame (`frame.test.ts`: once per form, `init(root)`, contract versions, model data of other shapes, the chunk on each kind of first touch, once per page, one try when it fails) and the reading of the page (`page.test.ts`: the post's names, the reading stage, a site's own type, server-resolved values, unreadable and over-limit answers, a group within and past its limits, the rules' answers, controls changed); The size check: core 0.99 KB of 16 KB, everything 7.08 KB of 40 KB. `scripts/ci-local.ps1` passed in 756 s (unit 7,353, 1 skipped, integration 875, client 2,733, browser 70); no product code of Core or Web changed, so Core's coverage stands as 6.4a measured it. Clean clones of a6ead9d and c12c8d8 each built with 0 warnings and passed the unit tests, the mutation list check and the vector files check; check-text, the secret scan and the name scan clean. Docs: ADR-0110; ADR-0004 and ADR-0109 point to it; `docs/architecture.md` (the runtime's line, the measured runtime budget); `tests/README.md` (the `posts` suite, the runtime's tests); `tests/e2e/README.md` (the website project). **Gotchas:** ESLint refuses non-null assertions in the client, so a lookup that must find something says so with a type guard or a helper that throws; the browser's table of the built-in types decides a repeatable group, since every projected field carries a `fields` array, empty or not; a C# mutant must still compile (a parameter left unused is IDE0060, an unreachable `default` CS0162, a field left unread IDE0052); a mutant whose case makes billions of rows runs Vitest out of memory, which the mutation check counts as broken, not caught.

A signed request carries `ServerKeyRequest` (7.1 and 7.2 read it to skip the challenge, honeypot and captcha) and counts in its key's partition; its submission records the key's name as `jfbServerKey` (never a posted one), the digest holds it so only the same key's retry matches (another key's is `409 duplicate_submission`), and the receipt answers that key and `404` to another. Context names starting with `jfb` are reserved (publishing refuses them). `scripts/server-key.mjs` makes a key and its hash. The origin check stays: a browser cannot send the header across origins. Checks: unit 7,417 (`ServerKeysTests` 17), integration `PublicServerKeyTests` 3 and the public API's 101, script tests 3, the gate passed in 914 s in a clone.

- 2026-10-31 · 6.8a The review's fixes to the public API and the post · f2114d3 · The seven things the phase review found in the public API and the post without JavaScript, each with its ADR amended. **Context names** (ADR-0128): the element endpoint drops a `context-<name>` the form does not list before the rendering, whose log-once set kept every name it had not seen, so a request's made-up names are neither kept nor logged. **Receipts** (ADR-0126): a receipt asked without a server key no longer answers an entry a key made, and an entry no key made answers only a request without one; every other request meets the same `404 not_found`. **Unique answers** (ADR-0091): checked once every other answer and place passes, before the unit and inside it (a full place is then told alone), so a post that stores nothing answers alike whether an address is taken or free, on `entries` and on the post without JavaScript; the price is a second round for a visitor whose post also had other errors. **Files** (ADR-0102): the post without JavaScript checks the form's availability before it stores a file or a typed signature, and a closed or full form shows its notice whatever the button. **Members** (ADR-0100): another origin's post is refused `403` while a member is signed in, as for a form for members, since the member's cookie would make it their entry with the values their prefill hides; an anonymous post and one without an `Origin` pass. **Server keys** (ADR-0126, ADR-0042): read once, as every host option is, so a changed or revoked key takes effect at the next start and passes the start-up checks first. **`Rendering:IncludeAssetsAutomatically`** (§4.8, ADR-0093): built (`FormBuilderRenderingOptions`, true by default, its schema entry regenerated), the default of `<jfb-render assets>` (now `bool?`) and of the view component's `assets` (now `bool? = null`) when the placement does not say; the element endpoint keeps its explicit `false`. **Found on the way:** the first test of a changed key set it on the site's configuration, which the harness chains into a configuration every host shares, so later fixtures met a key without a name (in memory only, nothing on disk); the test now changes the options through a change token and a post-configure of its host's own, and tests/README.md says how. **Checks:** `FormElementEndpointTests` (1,000 unlisted names in ten requests: no warning, the listed one renders), `PublicServerKeyTests` (a receipt without a key, an unkeyed entry's receipt, a changed key needing a restart) and the new `PublicServerKeyStartTests` (a key in place of its hash stops the start, naming the setting and never the value; `Support/RecordingStartupValidator`, now shared with `SiteOptionSourceTakenNameTests`), `PublicFormUniqueTests` (two existing cases follow the new order; a taken and a free address beside an empty required field answer alike, nothing stored), `FormPostTests` (the same on the post without JavaScript, `post-unique.cshtml`; `.Files`: a file to a form that filled up, on Send and on a row's button, and that closed, stores nothing and shows the notice; `.Prefill`: another origin's post as the member `403`, nothing stored, an anonymous one and one without an `Origin` sent), the new `FormRenderingAssetsOffTests` (the site starts with the section; the tag helper and the view component write no assets, `assets="true"` and `assets = true` write them once, `<jfb-assets>` still writes them), and unit `FormBuilderOptionsTests` and `AppSettingsSchemaTests`. **Tamper checks:** 11 changes put back wrong, each with its own build: names unfiltered, a keyless receipt answering a keyed entry, unique answers beside every error and beside answers only, files before availability, a foreign origin as the member, every foreign origin refused, the keys read on every request, the view component and the tag helper ignoring the option, an asked-for asset ignored: all 11 caught (two first did not build, as the analyzers flagged what they left unused, and were rewritten). **Gate:** `node scripts/gate-clone.mjs run f2114d3` passed in 1,004 s (ci-local 1,002 s; unit 7,489 passed and 1 skipped, integration 966, client 3,067, Playwright 109). Scans: secret scan and check-text clean on the 32 staged files, the term guard without a list as before, the name scan 0 hits.
